The constitutional claims retain their own elements and are not subject to a universal racial-predominance or electoral-defeat requirement.

##### Robertson v. Bartels — racial composition and incumbent protection

**History and docket placement.** The three-judge District of New Jersey granted summary judgment on the redistricting counts May 31, 2001, explaining its ruling June 18, **148 F. Supp. 2d 443**. The Supreme Court summarily affirmed January 22, 2002, No. 01-721, without explanatory reasoning or recorded individual votes. The separate opinion at 150 F. Supp. 2d 691 concerns candidate residency. This entry proposes direct merits consideration of the redistricting judgment only. [Redistricting opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/FSupp2/148/443/2567884/); [Supreme Court order](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/012202.ZOR.html).

**Questions.** Does racial territorial design, including alleged protection of minority incumbents' electoral prospects, establish an independent constitutional deprivation? May *Page* bind different plaintiffs merely because they share political objectives and seek similar relief?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm on the alternative merits ground while declining to endorse the broad virtual-representation rationale. Leave the separately referred residency count outside the mandate.

**Reasoning.** Simulated *Shaw*, *Miller*, and *Easley* distinguish racial territorial design from exclusion, intentional dilution, unequal participation, or another independent wrong. Assume racial motivation for the legal question: the documented complaint concerns the composition of a common electorate and resulting reelection prospects, not separate racial ballot qualifications or unequal participation rights. Count II therefore needs express analysis, but does not escape the territorial rule merely by describing the preferred district as an incumbent benefit. Independently, simulated *Richards* rejects binding absent persons through shared interests alone; genuine representation, control, consent, or another sufficient relationship is required. [Richards v. Jefferson County](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/517/793/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Protection applies equally to voters of every race. The absence of a right to reelection does not excuse an otherwise unlawful racial allocation; the decisive question is the alleged injury's character. A supported, preserved independent deprivation must receive its own inquiry. Explain why the reviewed merits ground suffices without approving nonparty preclusion or adjudicating the residency controversy.

##### Balderas v. Texas — review the court-drawn map under the correct capacity rule

**History and docket placement.** After Texas failed to produce a timely congressional map for its thirty-two seats, the three-judge Eastern District of Texas adopted Plan 1151C on November 14, 2001. Direct appeal No. 01-1196 was docketed February 19, 2002; the historical Court summarily affirmed June 17, 2002, 536 U.S. 919. The proposed entry is direct-appeal screening. The separate state House and Senate judgments are not part of this congressional appeal. [Congressional opinion](https://redistricting.lls.edu/wp-content/uploads/TX-balderas-20011114-congress.pdf); [official docket](https://www.supremecourt.gov/search.aspx?filename=/docketfiles/01-1196.htm); [historical orders and context](https://www.ncsl.org/redistricting-and-census/2000s-redistricting-case-summaries).

**Questions.** Did rejection of additional Latino opportunity districts use an impermissible numerical-majority screen? Do independent compactness or totality findings nevertheless sustain the judgment, and which claims and supporting evidence were preserved on appeal?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Retain the appeal and request focused supplemental briefing on preservation, functional-capacity evidence, and the independence of the compactness and totality findings. Make no present liability determination, replacement-map order, or election injunction. The jurisdictional statement's full text and relevant record extracts remain necessary before choosing an unconditional merits disposition.

**Reasoning.** Simulated *Growe* permits federal replacement when timely state processes fail; *White v. Weiser* and *Connor v. Finch* constrain the remedy. Judicial authorship does not insulate a map from §2. The opinion cites the historical Fifth Circuit's numerical-majority *Valdespino* rule, inconsistent with the simulated Supreme Court's dependable-crossover route. But the court also found compactness and totality deficiencies. Those findings cannot be discarded because one cited standard was wrong. Rejection of an unproved Black–Latino combination likewise does not automatically resolve a preserved single-group crossover claim. [Growe](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/507/25/); [White](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/412/783/); [Connor](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/431/407/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply both lawful capacity routes where supported by a preserved single-group claim. Affirm if an independent lawful deficiency disposes of it; otherwise remand only the affected claim for the correct inquiry. Neither route creates a required district count, an unpleaded coalition claim, or an automatic right to reopen the trial. Existing map orders remain operative pending lawful modification; any interim change requires the actual timetable and equitable findings.

##### Black v. McGuffage — unequal counting procedures as a background vehicle

**History and docket placement.** On March 29, 2002, the Northern District of Illinois allowed §2, equal-protection, and substantive-due-process claims concerning voting equipment and error notification to proceed, while dismissing privileges or immunities and rejecting the county clerks' immunity defense, 209 F. Supp. 2d 889. This was a pleading decision. No Supreme Court vehicle is established; counsel later reported a December 2003 settlement. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/FSupp2/209/889/2425165/); [settlement history, pp. 60–61](https://assets.aclu.org/live/uploads/publications/aclu_2019_report_to_congress_on_the_voting_rights_act_final_for_submission.pdf).

**Questions.** Do named voters allege concrete continuing exposure to deficient counting? Which statutory and constitutional claims can proceed, and are the responsible clerks acting locally for immunity purposes?

**Stone’s recommended use.** Retain as a lower-court access comparator, with no Supreme Court vote assigned. On the described pleading question, the recommended assessment permits §2, equality, and fundamental-unfairness claims to proceed, rejects the unsupported privileges-or-immunities theory, and rejects the clerks' immunity defense on the local-function facts.

**Reasoning.** Alleged exposure to identifiable continuing defects need not include identification of a particular lost secret ballot. Simulated *Lujan* still requires personal injury, traceability, and redressability. Residual-vote disparities warrant investigation but include possible voluntary abstention and do not prove lost lawful votes. Section 2 and its definition of voting reach casting and proper counting; *Chisom* supports coverage, while *Mosley* supplies a narrower historical analogy concerning deliberate failure to count. Simulated *Bush v. Gore*, *Burdick*, and *Yoshina* provide distinct equality, burden, and fundamental-unfairness principles. [Chisom](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/501/380/); [Mosley](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/238/383/); [Reynolds](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/377/533).

**What the opinion must articulate.** If a lawful Supreme Court vehicle arises, expressly justify applying §2 to counting inequality without district-formation prerequisites and extending *Bush* to materially unjustified disparities in initial counting. Do not imply those applications were already decided. Different machines alone do not violate equality; due process requires fundamental unfairness, and racial effects alone do not establish discriminatory purpose. Analyze immunity by the relevant governmental function. Any ultimate injunction must correct proved defects while allowing adequate lawful methods, not impose identical equipment or a new election on allegations alone.

#### October Term 2002

##### Cano v. Davis — intentional interference with participation and coalition-building

**History and docket placement.** The three-judge Central District of California granted summary judgment June 12, 2002, 211 F. Supp. 2d 1208; the historical Court summarily affirmed January 13, 2003, 537 U.S. 1100, No. 02-577. Proposed entry is direct-appeal screening followed by briefing on the preserved constitutional effects question. The lower court assumed the challenged intent evidence favorably to plaintiffs and rested intentional dilution on insufficient effect, not a dispositive finding that discriminatory purpose was absent. [Opinion](https://hallapproved.com/ca/cases/federal/district/2002/2572233/); [official docket](https://www.supremecourt.gov/search.aspx?filename=/docketfiles/02-577.htm); [historical affirmance](https://clearinghouse-umich-production.s3.amazonaws.com/media/doc/67827.pdf).

**Questions.** Can officials intentionally impair Latino voters' actual participation or realistic coalition-building to protect incumbents even when preferred candidates retain electoral success? Separately, do the CD28 and SD27 results claims establish legally consequential opposing bloc voting, and does CD51 present an injury beyond preferred district placement?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reject summary affirmance of the entire judgment and obtain plenary briefing. Assuming the CD28 constitutional effects question remains within the appeal, affirm the CD28/CD51 design-only judgments; affirm the CD28/SD27 §2 results judgments and intentional statutory variants on the asserted injury; vacate and remand the CD28 intentional constitutional ruling; and affirm the CD51 constitutional judgment as presented. No immediate redraw or liability finding follows.

**Reasoning.** CD28's alleged racial reduction targeted primary competition. Success in other contests does not conclusively answer whether that action materially impaired the affected electorate's organization and realistic coalition opportunity. *White v. Regester* examines practical political access; *Anderson v. Celebrezze* explains electoral association without itself deciding districting. *Garza* demonstrates that incumbency protection can operate through intentional racial fragmentation, while its stronger record does not prove these plaintiffs' case. The statutory claims remain separate: the district court found triable first and second *Gingles* conditions but insufficient local third-condition evidence, so a numerical-capacity remand is unwarranted. CD51's conceded internal opportunity and excluded voters' placement preference do not establish impaired electoral activity. [White](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/412/755); [Anderson](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/460/780); [Garza](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F2/918/763/24305/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Announce the new equal-protection application expressly: deliberate racial district assignments cannot materially impair identified voters' actual association, grounded coalition-building opportunities, or electoral participation, including primary activity. Require purpose, concrete impairment, and causation; neither an existing winning district, impossible victory, nor a fixed election count is essential. Mere influence or preferred boundaries are insufficient. Apply *Arlington Heights* and the *Mt. Healthy* same-decision defense. The remand must address admissibility and disputed facts without inventing a First Amendment count or reviving the expressly unasserted influence claim. Any remedy removes the proved impediment and gives California the initial opportunity to correct it; it does not prescribe a racial percentage or winner. [Arlington Heights](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/252); [Mt. Healthy](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/274).

##### Montiel v. Davis — separate population, constitutional, and statutory proof

**History and docket placement.** The three-judge Southern District of Alabama granted summary judgment July 8, 2002, 215 F. Supp. 2d 1279. Senate and House maximum population deviations were 9.78% and 9.93%. OT2002 direct review is conditional preparation, not a verified Supreme Court appeal. Congressional and education-board claims had been severed. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/FSupp2/215/1279/2503718/).

**Questions.** Do deviations below ten percent nevertheless reflect purposeful unequal weighting? Do racial demographic patterns and county splits establish a constitutional deprivation or an independently supported §2 injury to white voters?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** If a proper appeal supplies a record consistent with the published account, affirm for insufficient proof. Verify any developed statutory claim disclosed by the appellate papers before extending that disposition to it. No replacement map follows.

**Reasoning.** Simulated *Voinovich*, together with *Reynolds* and *Brown v. Thomson*, distinguishes failure to establish a violation through disparity alone from immunity against evidence of unlawful weighting. Here the reported proof did not move beyond population figures, boundaries, alternatives, and suppositions. Simulated *Shaw*, *Miller*, and *Easley* likewise reject racial design alone while preserving actual impairment. Section 2 remains an independent results claim protecting citizens of every race; failure of a design claim cannot decide statutory liability. [Reynolds](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/377/533/); [Brown](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/462/835/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Below-ten-percent deviations are challengeable. Reject the lower opinion's quoted “solely” formulation as a universal requirement to prove an exclusively improper motive; affirm only if the evidentiary deficiency independently sustains judgment. Political explanations cannot immunize proved unequal weighting or intentional racial impairment. Preserve district-specific personal injury, independent §2 analysis, and equal protection for white and Black voters; leave the severed cases outside the mandate.

##### Old Person v. Brown — corrected totality and the distinction between liability and relief

**History and docket placement.** The Ninth Circuit affirmed Montana's non-dilution judgment December 4, 2002, 312 F.3d 1036. The entry is hypothetical OT2002 certiorari screening; no filed petition, grant, or historical Supreme Court disposition is verified. The panel corrected the district court's proportionality analysis and rejected approaching redistricting as an alternative liability bar. [Opinion](https://openjurist.org/312/f3d/1036/old-person-v-brown); [district findings](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/FSupp2/182/1002/2576375/).

**Questions.** After the *Gingles* conditions are satisfied, does the corrected totality assessment support judgment against the four remaining Native American plaintiffs? How much weight belongs to electoral success, an unusual contest, and disproportionality? Can an anticipated replacement map defeat otherwise established liability?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** No certiorari vote is assigned without a petition and updated justiciability record. If merits review occurs in a live controversy, affirm on the corrected totality assessment, with no injunction or mandated additional district; reject the district court's proportionality calculation and timing rationale.

**Reasoning.** Simulated *De Grandy* preserves an independent totality inquiry after the causal prerequisites. Discrimination, polarization, socioeconomic barriers, racial appeals, and corrected disproportionality favor plaintiffs. Preferred-candidate victories in both relevant House districts and one Senate district remain evidence alongside other supported findings, not a numerical safe harbor. Joey Jayne's 2000 victory against only a third-party opponent cannot establish durable equal opportunity by itself. The circuit accounted for the legal corrections and nevertheless found the ultimate assessment permissible under clear-error review. The United States' amicus participation concerned remedy, not an endorsement of the fact-dependent liability claim. [De Grandy](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/512/997/); [United States brief](https://www.justice.gov/sites/default/files/crt/legacy/2010/12/14/old.pdf).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Electoral success neither cancels bloc voting nor automatically defeats liability; its weight depends on the circumstances. Broader geographic evidence may illuminate plaintiffs' own-district injuries without conferring standing everywhere. Future redistricting affects available relief and possible mootness, not whether an existing arrangement violated §2. Affirming this corrected factual assessment creates no universal geographic frame, proportionality threshold, or prohibition on special elections.

##### Georgia v. Ashcroft — effective opportunity, enforcement power, and intervention

**History and docket placement.** On direct merits review after the April 29, 2003 argument, Georgia sought approval of its original 2001 Senate plan despite separate clearance of a revised Senate plan. The historical Court vacated and remanded June 26, 2003, 539 U.S. 461, allowing broader consideration of influence and statewide political power; it also sustained private intervention. The House, congressional, and revised-Senate clearances were not contested. [Historical majority](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/02-182.ZO.html); [historical dissent](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/02-182.ZD.html); [contemporaneous federal brief](https://www.justice.gov/osg/brief/georgia-v-ashcroft-brief-merits).

**Questions.** Did Georgia prove that its original Senate plan would not diminish effective opportunity relative to the lawful benchmark? Can influence or broader coalition opportunities compensate for an established loss? Is that application of §5 within congressional enforcement power, and may affected voters intervene in judicial preclearance?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm denial of preclearance to the original Senate plan. Reject the as-applied constitutional objection and sustain the four voters' intervention under Rule 24. Georgia's continuing desire to use the original plan keeps that controversy live; the voters' continuing defense of its rejection preserves the party-status issue. Leave the other clearances undisturbed and order no replacement map.

**Reasoning.** *Beer* supplies nonretrogression against the operative 1997 benchmark, and simulated *Bossier* preserves distinct effects and purpose inquiries. Georgia bore the burden of proving nonretrogression. District-specific polarization evidence undermined its statewide estimate that 44.3% Black voting-age population generally ensured opportunity; legislative support could not replace the missing local demonstration for Districts 2, 12, and 26. Lower racial percentages remain lawful where dependable crossover preserves opportunity. *City of Rome*, preserved by simulated *Boerne*, permits preventive effects regulation without a new constitutional violation in every submitted change. For intervention, §5's judicial route does not displace Rule 24; *NAACP v. New York* applies ordinary intervention principles, while *Morris v. Gressette* concerns administrative preclearance. [Beer](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/425/130/); [City of Rome](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/446/156/); [NAACP](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/413/345/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Announce expressly that predicted influence, sympathetic officeholders, committee positions, or hoped-for policy gains cannot replace reliable electoral agency that the change removes. Rearranging districts or creating several genuine coalition opportunities is permissible if evidence shows preserved or improved agency for the affected voters; statewide counts alone do not suffice. Apply the ordinary preponderance burden, not certainty or guaranteed victory. The broader simulated *Bossier* purpose rule remains controlling, but failure on effects makes a fresh purpose finding unnecessary. Keep §2 and §5 distinct, uphold preventive enforcement without racial maximization or frozen boundaries, and explain why intervention does not reopen unappealed clearances. Apply the statute then in force, without importing the 2006 amendments.

##### Wilkins v. West — state protections and potentially interwoven federal standards

**History and docket placement.** On November 1, 2002, the Supreme Court of Virginia vacated portions of the trial judgment for lack of standing, reversed the remaining invalidations, and entered judgment for defendants, 264 Va. 447, 571 S.E.2d 100. Retain the case as a state-court comparator; no Supreme Court petition or merits proceeding is verified. [Opinion](https://caselaw.findlaw.com/court/va-supreme-court/1319316.html); [state opinion text](https://law.justia.com/cases/virginia/supreme-court/2002/1021003.html).

**Questions.** Which voters may challenge which districts, and do Virginia's compactness, contiguity, and racial-discrimination protections invalidate the map? Does the racial ruling rest on an independently sufficient state ground or a potentially reviewable federal premise?

**Stone’s recommended use.** Keep as background comparison, with no Supreme Court merits or certiorari vote and no federal injunction, redraw, or remand assigned. A later petition requires its own federal-question and preservation review.

**Reasoning.** The racial ruling expressly treated federal equal-protection standards as congruent with Virginia's Article I, §11. Its state constitutional label therefore does not by itself establish independence. *Michigan v. Long*, simulated *Middlebrooks*, and simulated *Ino Ino* require examining whether a preserved federal premise matters to the result. Virginia's separate compactness and contiguity protections remain state questions. Simulated *Sinkfield* requires personal exposure for federal standing, while *Shaw*, *Miller*, and *Easley* reject territorial predominance alone and preserve actually established deprivation. [Long](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/463/1032/); [28 U.S.C. §1257](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/28/1257).

**What the opinion must articulate.** No holding follows from a background entry. If federal review becomes available, distinguish issue-specific state independence from an interwoven federal premise; neither a state citation nor disagreement with state reasoning resolves jurisdiction. Preserve stronger independent state protection, avoid converting state standing or compactness rules into federal commands, and require inquiry into any preserved independent injury. A partisan explanation supplies no immunity from proved racial impairment, and this entry does not affirm the statewide map's constitutionality.

#### October Term 2003

##### Vieth v. Jubelirer — establish the proposed partisan-injury rule

**History and docket placement.** Supreme Court, April 28, 2004, 541 U.S. 267. The real judgment affirmed dismissal; four Justices would make partisan-gerrymandering claims nonjusticiable. Act 1 governed the 2002 election after a stay; Act 34 was its prospective replacement. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/02-1580.ZO.html); [procedural history](https://www.ncsl.org/redistricting-and-census/2000s-redistricting-case-summaries).

**Questions.** Are partisan-districting claims justiciable, and what personal injury and causal showing make them judicially enforceable? Which map is operative, and which lawful plan supplies the comparison?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reject categorical nonjusticiability; vacate and remand under Foreclose’s representation and association branches, permitting conforming amendment under ordinary procedure. Entrench does not cover this congressional delegation. Require a claim reaching the operative map and proof of any asserted continuity. Act 1’s adjudged population defect cannot make it a lawful baseline; a stay permitting an election does not resolve that problem. The remand does not establish liability.

**Reasoning.** *Baker v. Carr*, 369 U.S. 186 (1962), and *Davis v. Bandemer*, 478 U.S. 109 (1986), support judicial examination; *Gaffney v. Cummings*, 412 U.S. 735 (1973), permits ordinary political considerations. Representation injury requires inability to elect in every destination throughout the recorded range over the prior lawful map’s life, rather than reduced chances or proportional-seat claims. An emerging electorate may establish opportunity through an independently grounded lawful comparison; its present evidence must be reliably connected to that range. Association injury requires a proved mechanism materially impairing actual political activity or concrete efforts to organize or participate; a harder election alone is insufficient, and no separate duration or election gate applies. No new-map election is mandatory. These precedents support comparisons and limits, not the exact proposed test; Stone must defend the new districting rules expressly. [Baker](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/369/186/); [Bandemer](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/109/); [Gaffney](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/412/735/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Announce the proposed Foreclose rule expressly, with representation and association as distinct injuries, rather than attributing its precise thresholds to earlier cases. Define the recorded range, independent lawful comparisons, continuity and the emerging-electorate route; impose no fixed election count. Respect binding law and genuine state criteria without making those criteria the exclusive source of a comparison. Protect actual association and concrete organizing efforts without a separate duration gate. Reserve Entrench for a chamber-control vehicle and exclude congressional delegations. Remand only live, preserved claims or amendments permitted by ordinary procedure, with purpose, causation and the same-decision defense addressed and no finding of liability.

##### Martinez v. Bush — hold for Vieth, then separate the theories

**History and docket placement.** Three-judge S.D. Fla., December 3, 2002, 234 F. Supp. 2d 1275. Proposed OT2003 review would be held for the proposed simulated decision in *Vieth*. The real district court rejected challenges to Florida's congressional and legislative plans. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/FSupp2/234/1275/2513250/).

**Questions.** Do the proved facts establish racial discrimination or statutory dilution? If the proposed partisan rule is adopted in Vieth, do the preserved congressional or legislative claims satisfy either applicable injury route?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the independently unsupported racial and statutory claims. If the proposed *Vieth* rule is adopted, vacate the preserved partisan portion for its application. Congressional claims need Foreclose; legislative claims may invoke either partisan operation if properly presented. The proposed rule imposes no completed-election waiting requirement.

**Reasoning.** Racial purpose is not inferred merely from party advantage, but party advantage cannot excuse racial injury actually proved; fictional *Easley* separates those propositions. *Gingles* governs statutory opportunity, while *Bandemer* illustrates the distinct partisan inquiry. Projected seat shares do not establish electorate-specific foreclosure, concrete association impairment, or chamber control retained throughout the recorded conditions in which the opposition has a reliably measured absolute vote majority. A record preceding implementation is not automatically premature. Remand applies the proposed standard, if adopted, without announcing that Florida’s plans violate it. [Gingles](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/30/); [Bandemer](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/109/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the independence of constitutional racial injury, statutory opportunity and partisan injury. Apply the rule proposed for Vieth only if adopted: congressional claims may invoke Foreclose; legislative claims may also invoke Entrench. Require actual association or concrete organizing activity, without a separate duration or election gate, and allow independently grounded lawful comparisons for emerging electorates. Affirm the independently unsupported racial and statutory claims; confine remand to the preserved partisan claims and procedurally available amendments.

##### Cox v. Larios — population favoritism is independently unlawful

**History and docket placement.** Supreme Court summary affirmance, June 30, 2004, 542 U.S. 947, affirming 300 F. Supp. 2d 1320. Georgia's legislative plans systematically used population deviations to favor particular regions and Democratic incumbents. [Supreme Court disposition and writings](https://flexlaw.co/case/674957/2004-cox-v-larios-542-u-s-947).

**Questions.** Can legislative population deviations below ten percent violate equal representation when systematically used to favor regions or incumbents, and what legitimate state policies could justify those deviations?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm on one-person, one-vote grounds.

**Reasoning.** Tolerating minor deviations allows legitimate state policies to operate; it does not permit the State to weight votes systematically for the favored party or region. *Reynolds*, 377 U.S. 533 (1964), supplies equal representation; *Gaffney*, 412 U.S. 735 (1973), permits political awareness and balanced accommodation, not the unexplained one-sided population favoritism proved here. *Brown v. Thomson*, 462 U.S. 835 (1983), makes a sub-10% deviation insufficient by itself to establish a violation, not an absolute safe harbor. This case therefore needs neither Foreclose nor Entrench and remains actionable even if those operations have not been adopted. [Reynolds](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/377/533/); [Gaffney](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/412/735/); [Brown](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/462/835/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify that a deviation below ten percent is not an absolute safe harbor and that political awareness does not authorize systematic population favoritism. Apply population-equality doctrine to the actual pattern and asserted justifications. Reserve the proposed partisan operations because this independent constitutional ground resolves the case. Affirm the judgment and its necessary corrective relief without converting every minor deviation into a violation.

##### Duckworth v. State Administration Board of Election Laws — pleading and the three-judge court

**History and docket placement.** Fourth Circuit, June 19, 2003, 332 F.3d 769; proposed OT2003 certiorari review. The actual decision affirmed dismissal by a single judge, treating failure to state a partisan claim as insufficient substantiality for a three-judge court. [Opinion](https://openjurist.org/332/f3d/769/duckworth-v-state-administration-board-of-election-laws).

**Questions.** May a single judge dismiss a substantial constitutional redistricting claim on its merits instead of convening the statutory three-judge court? What allegations remain necessary under the partisan rule proposed for Vieth?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Vacate and remand for the proper three-judge procedure if the federal claim is substantial; allow that court to apply the proposed *Vieth* rule if adopted.

**Reasoning.** An allegation can fail on its merits without being so frivolous that a single judge may bypass the statutory panel. *Goosby v. Osser*, 409 U.S. 512 (1973), already supplies that distinction; the later *Shapiro* decision in 2015 is unnecessary as entering authority. On substance, bizarre boundaries alone establish neither representation foreclosure, concrete association impairment, nor Entrench. The procedural remand does not imply a winning partisan claim. Apply any adopted partisan rule through the correct court and ordinary pleading procedure. [Goosby](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/409/512/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the narrow distinction between an insubstantial federal claim and a substantial claim that may fail under the governing merits rule. Direct the required panel procedure for a substantial claim; leave that court to apply any adopted Vieth standard and ordinary amendment rules. Reserve the merits and remedy until the proper court assesses the alleged injury. Bizarre boundaries alone do not become a substantive violation through this procedural ruling.

##### Valeria v. Davis — a curricular change is not automatically racial restructuring

**History and docket placement.** Ninth Circuit, October 7, 2002, 307 F.3d 1036; proposed OT2003 merits review. The decision upheld Proposition 227 against the facial political-process equal-protection challenge. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F3/307/1036/521867/).

**Questions.** Does Proposition 227 selectively restructure the political process on a racial basis or obstruct otherwise available remedies for racial discrimination, rather than merely replace one educational policy?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm that confined judgment; preserve actual federal educational duties and supported as-applied claims.

**Reasoning.** Fictional *Coalition for Economic Equity v. Wilson* condemns selective barriers to otherwise available racial remedies, not every statewide withdrawal of a favored policy. *Hunter v. Erickson*, 393 U.S. 385 (1969), and *Washington v. Seattle School District*, 458 U.S. 457 (1982), require attention to whether the change specially reallocates racial remedial authority. On the findings here, replacing bilingual instruction with English immersion does not by itself establish that mechanism or purposeful racial disadvantage. *Lau v. Nichols*, 414 U.S. 563 (1974), preserves the separate duty to provide meaningful educational access; it does not constitutionalize one instructional method. [Hunter](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/393/385/); [Seattle](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/458/457/); [Lau](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/414/563/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the discriminatory reallocation of authority necessary for the political-process claim and distinguish it from the withdrawal of a preferred instructional method. Apply the supported findings without presuming racial purpose from disparate effects. Reserve federal educational-access duties and supported as-applied claims. Affirm only the judgment rejecting this facial political-process challenge; do not authorize noncompliance with those independent duties.

##### Rubin v. City of Santa Monica — neutral ballot descriptions

**History and docket placement.** Ninth Circuit, October 17, 2002, 308 F.3d 1008; proposed OT2003 merits review. The city could refuse “peace activist” as an occupational ballot designation under its neutral rule. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F3/308/1008/559114/).

**Questions.** May the city neutrally exclude “peace activist” from occupational ballot descriptions without violating speech or electoral rights, and when would enforcement instead become an ideological penalty?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm, limited to neutral enforcement of the designation rule.

**Reasoning.** A ballot identifies choices; it need not carry every candidate's preferred advocacy message. *Burdick v. Takushi*, 504 U.S. 428 (1992), supplies the burden-and-justification framework, and *Timmons v. Twin Cities Area New Party*, 520 U.S. 351 (1997), explains why a ballot is not an unrestricted forum. The modest burden is supported by orderly, accurate descriptions and remaining channels for campaigning. Distinguish fictional *Cook v. Gralike*: government may regulate descriptive form but may not use its official ballot to penalize an ideological position. A selectively enforced rule would present a different claim. [Burdick](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/504/428/); [Timmons](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/520/351/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the distinction between a neutral description rule and government manipulation of the ballot to disadvantage a viewpoint. Apply the actual-burden and justification inquiry, including remaining campaign channels. Reserve selective-enforcement and materially different designation claims. Affirm the confined application without endorsing compelled ideology or a general power to suppress candidate advocacy.

##### Arakaki v. Hawaii — ancestry restrictions on public candidacy

**History and docket placement.** Ninth Circuit, December 31, 2002, 314 F.3d 1091; proposed OT2003 merits review. The appellate court invalidated the ancestry condition for elected Office of Hawaiian Affairs trustees and vacated the appointment ruling for lack of standing. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F3/314/1091/531764/).

**Questions.** Does an ancestry qualification for elected public trustees violate equal protection even when the electorate is open to all voters? Do the plaintiffs have standing to challenge the separate appointment restriction?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm both the elected-office result and the standing limitation; decide candidacy expressly rather than pretending fictional *Rice* already resolved it.

**Reasoning.** Use the Fourteenth Amendment’s protection against ancestry-based exclusion from public office: opening the vote does not cure a racial restriction on eligibility to serve. *Rice v. Cayetano*, 528 U.S. 495 (2000), supplies the public-election setting but reserved candidacy; *Smith v. Allwright*, 321 U.S. 649 (1944), is an analogy about public political participation, not a holding that the Fifteenth Amendment directly governs every candidate qualification. No proof that the excluded candidate would win is necessary to identify unequal civic status. This does not decide sovereign tribal membership or all Native Hawaiian programs. The proposed extension of racial Exclude to candidacy must be adopted expressly. [Rice](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/528/495/); [Smith](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/321/649/).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Announce expressly the proposed application of racial Exclude to eligibility for this public elected office under the Fourteenth Amendment. Explain that exclusion from candidacy is the injury without requiring proof that the claimant would win. Preserve the separate standing dismissal for appointments. Reserve sovereign tribal membership, other Native Hawaiian programs and any broader Fifteenth Amendment candidacy theory. Affirm the elected-office relief and the appointment standing limitation.

#### October Term 2004

##### Jackson v. Perry, 543 U.S. 941 (2004)

**History and docket placement.** Actual Supreme Court order, October 18, 2004: the Texas congressional-redistricting judgment was vacated and remanded for reconsideration after *Vieth*. This belongs in OT2004. [Order](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/543/941/USSC_PRO_543_941_03-1391).

**Questions.** If Vieth adopts the proposed partisan standard, must the Texas congressional-redistricting judgment be reconsidered under it, and which preserved claims and evidence belong on remand?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Vacate the partisan disposition and remand under the rule proposed for simulated *Vieth*, if adopted, preserving separate statutory claims.

**Reasoning.** If simulated *Vieth* adopts the proposed partisan rule, the trial court should apply it to the affected voters and actual assignments. Partisan motive does not establish inability to elect across the recorded range or material impairment of actual association or concrete organizing efforts. [*Bandemer*, 478 U.S. 109 (1986)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/109/), supplies the earlier justiciability debate; [*Mt. Healthy*, 429 U.S. 274, 287 (1977)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/274), supplies the causation analogy. Prior opportunity and any claimed continuity require factual support, but there is no fixed election count. November 2004 returns may supplement the remand record; they were unavailable on October 18 and are not a prerequisite to suit.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify that remand depends on adoption of the proposed Vieth rule and does not find Texas liable. Require an identified electorate and lawful comparison for representation, or a concrete mechanism impairing actual association or organizing efforts, without an additional duration gate. Explain continuity and causation without a fixed election count. Preserve separate statutory claims; permit genuinely relevant later returns under ordinary procedure without treating them as a prerequisite to suit.

##### Black Political Task Force v. Galvin, 300 F. Supp. 2d 291 (D. Mass. 2004)

**History and docket placement.** The actual three-judge decision was February 24, 2004, in OT2003. Retain an OT2004 Supreme Court entry only as a **proposed appeal**, not an actual Supreme Court merits decision. The court found §2 liability affecting seventeen Boston-area House districts and allowed six weeks for a legislative remedy. [Opinion](https://openjurist.org/300/fsupp2d/291/black-political-task-force-v-galvin-2563017).

**Questions.** Did incumbent-protective packing deny Black voters equal statutory opportunity on the proved alternative districting and voting evidence? What remedy respects the legislature’s first opportunity to cure?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** If a timely appeal is posited, affirm the §2 judgment and the State-first remedial order.

**Reasoning.** Concentrating Black voters to protect incumbents can leave surrounding communities without equal electoral opportunity. Liability rests on the demonstrated compact alternative, cohesion, bloc voting, and total circumstances—not on boundaries or racial percentages alone. [*Gingles*, 478 U.S. 30, 50–51 (1986)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/478/30), provides the statutory inquiry; fictional *De Grandy* rejects maximization and numerical safe harbors. Resolve the proven African-American statutory claim without unnecessarily deciding the constitutional and Latino claims. Fictional *Growe* and *Lopez* support giving the legislature the first opportunity to cure.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify each statutory prerequisite, the independent totality inquiry and the connection between the proved packing and lost opportunity. Explain why neither a high racial percentage nor the possibility of another district alone establishes liability. Reserve unnecessary constitutional and Latino claims. If the proposed timely appeal is used, affirm the §2 judgment and the bounded opportunity for a legislative cure; require a judicial remedy only if a lawful state response is unavailable.

##### Spencer v. Blackwell, 347 F. Supp. 2d 528 (S.D. Ohio Nov. 1, 2004), followed by Spencer v. Pugh, 543 U.S. 1301 (Nov. 2, 2004) — emergency vehicle

**History and docket placement.** District courts restricted polling-place challengers after evidence of threatened delay and intimidation. The Sixth Circuit stayed their orders. Justice Stevens, acting alone as Circuit Justice hours before voting began, denied applications to vacate those stays for prudential reasons; he did not refer them to the full Court or adjudicate the merits. [District opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/FSupp2/347/528/2422028/); [in-chambers opinion](https://openjurist.org/543/us/1301/spencer-et-al-v-pugh-et-al).

**Questions.** Do the emergency records justify restoring limited protections against disruptive polling-place challenges despite the proximity of voting? Can that relief preserve lawful eligibility checks and be administered in time?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** No fictional merits judgment. **If the emergency applications are expressly reconstructed as referred to the Court**, Stone would vote to vacate the stays and restore the limited district-court protections for that election, reserving final liability.

**Reasoning.** The evidence concerned concrete interference with voting, not the mere presence of party observers. State-authorized challenges that foreseeably impose severe, unjustified obstacles require assessment under fictional *Burdick*. Retaining lawful qualification checks while restricting disruptive challenger activity protects both access and administration. [*Reynolds*, 377 U.S. at 585](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/377/533), makes election timing an equitable consideration, not a reason to disregard proved imminent harm. Stone should explain why restoring these already litigated protections is workable despite the hour. *Purcell* is a later comparison, unavailable to this 2004 opinion.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify that any Stone vote requires the expressly reconstructed referral of the emergency applications to the full Court. Identify the imminent voting injury, applicable stay standards, feasibility and competing harms; do not equate observers’ presence with intimidation. Reserve final liability and broader challenger restrictions. If the emergency prerequisites are established, vacate the stays and restore only the workable district-court protections. Use law available in 2004 rather than a later Purcell rule.

#### October Term 2005

##### League of United Latin American Citizens v. Perry, 548 U.S. 399 (2006)

**History and docket placement.** Actual Supreme Court decision, June 28, 2006. The real Court rejected statewide partisan challenges and District 24's §2 claim, but found §2 dilution in District 23. [Opinions and record discussion](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/548/399/).

**Questions.** Does District 23 unlawfully reduce Latino electoral opportunity, and does District 24 independently establish a §2 entitlement under the existing functional-capacity rule? Do preserved challenges to the congressional replacement map establish representation or association foreclosure?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reverse as to District 23. Affirm rejection of District 24’s §2 claim on the supported failure to prove dependable electoral opportunity, without imposing a numerical-majority requirement. If the proposed Foreclose rule is adopted, vacate and remand the preserved partisan claims under it.

**Reasoning.** Texas cannot answer District 23’s loss of Latino electoral opportunity merely by pointing elsewhere; [*Gingles*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/478/30) and fictional *De Grandy* require the affected community’s opportunity and the lawful alternative. District 24 presents a different failure of proof. The real opinion assumed a sub-50% claim could succeed but sustained findings against dependable electoral choice, including mixed expert results and conflicting testimony. Fictional *Valdespino* removes numerical exclusivity, not that factual burden. Stone can sustain the finding without equating candidate preference with candidate race or treating an uncontested primary as automatically disqualifying. [Actual opinion, Part IV](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/05-204.ZO.html).

For the partisan claims, no fixed number of prior or challenged-map elections is required under the proposed rule. The 2002 map’s brief life does not itself defeat demonstrated opportunity, and lawful-predecessor history may count upon proof of continuity. For representation, identify the same electorate and show inability to elect in every destination throughout the relevant recorded range. For association, identify actual political activity or concrete efforts to organize or participate, a mechanism materially impairing that activity, prohibited purpose, and causation; no separate duration or election gate applies. Incumbent losses alone prove neither branch. The absence of a new census does not eliminate every lawful reason for redrawing. [*Mt. Healthy*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/274) supports the causal safeguard, not a particular electoral threshold.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify Districts 23 and 24 separately under the statutory elements, existing fictional Valdespino and totality. Explain why another district elsewhere cannot cure a proved local injury and why numerical minority status alone cannot defeat a functional claim. Apply the proposed partisan rule only if adopted, including recorded conditions, continuity, lawful comparisons and actual association or concrete organizing efforts without duration or election gates. Reserve Entrench because these are congressional districts. Order a fitted District 23 cure, sustain the supported District 24 finding and confine partisan remand to preserved claims.

##### Johnson v. Governor of Florida, 405 F.3d 1214 (11th Cir. 2005) (en banc)

**History and docket placement.** En banc judgment April 12, 2005; certiorari denied November 14, 2005, in OT2005. A merits opinion here requires a **hypothetical limited grant**. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F3/405/1214/473541/); [certiorari history](https://www.brennancenter.org/our-work/court-cases/johnson-v-bush).

**Questions.** Does the operative 1968 criminal-disenfranchisement provision violate equal protection because of its historical antecedents despite the record concerning the reenactment? Is the separate §2 question within the proposed grant?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Grant only the equal-protection question and affirm its rejection on this record; leave the separate §2 question outside the grant.

**Reasoning.** [*Hunter v. Underwood*, 471 U.S. 222 (1985)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/471/222/), forbids racially motivated criminal disenfranchisement; it does not make every later enactment permanently unlawful. The operative 1968 provision narrowed the excluded class, and the plaintiffs stipulated that there was no evidence of racial motivation in that reenactment. Fictional *Brooks* supports evaluating the operative decision and causation. Neither new wording nor voter approval automatically cleanses discrimination, but no ritual acknowledgment of past wrongdoing is required. This narrow affirmance neither approves every felon-disqualification rule nor decides whether §2 independently reaches this one.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify that the operative enactment, its actual purpose and the applicable causal inquiry control; neither permanent taint nor automatic cleansing follows from reenactment. Explain the stipulated absence of evidence of racial motivation in 1968 and the provision’s narrowing effect. Reserve §2 outside the hypothetical limited grant and avoid approving all criminal-disenfranchisement rules. Affirm only the equal-protection disposition on this record.

#### October Term 2006

##### Kidd v. Cox, 2006 WL 1341302 (N.D. Ga. May 16, 2006)

**History and docket placement.** Three-judge district court, May 16, 2006; OT2006 Supreme Court review is **hypothetical**. The court rejected population claims, treated the partisan equal-protection claim as nonjusticiable, and rejected the First Amendment theory. [NCSL's case account](https://www.ncsl.org/redistricting-and-census/2000s-redistricting-case-summaries); [Federal Judicial Center procedural history](https://www.fjc.gov/sites/default/files/materials/09/EE-GAN-1-06-cv-997-Kidd.pdf).

**Questions.** Are the preserved partisan claims judicially cognizable under the rule proposed for Vieth, and what concrete injury could the county split or targeting of a candidate establish? Do the population claims independently warrant relief?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Leave the population disposition intact. Vacate the categorical partisan dismissal and remand under the proposed *Vieth* rule if adopted, allowing procedurally proper conforming pleading. No completed-election prerequisite applies to either proposed partisan operation.

**Reasoning.** Splitting Athens-Clarke County or targeting Kidd does not establish representation foreclosure, material impairment of actual association or concrete organizing efforts, or chamber-wide Entrench. The proposed rule permits competent proof before voting and does not require a fixed number of prior victories. An emerging electorate may establish real opportunity through a lawful, workable comparison with independent legal and practical roots, without prior electoral success; preference for a friendlier district is insufficient. *Bandemer* supplies the justiciability antecedent; [*Gaffney v. Cummings*, 412 U.S. 735 (1973)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/412/735/), establishes that political awareness alone is insufficient. The remand is not a merits win from the county split.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify that targeting a candidate or dividing a county does not alone prove either partisan injury. If the proposed Vieth rule is adopted, permit representation, association or legislative Entrench claims on their respective elements without a mandatory completed-election count. Include concrete organizing efforts, no separate association duration gate, and an independently grounded lawful comparison for an emerging electorate. Preserve the population judgment and remand only the partisan dismissal through proper pleading procedure; order no map change without a proved violation and remedy findings.

##### Bone Shirt v. Hazeltine, 461 F.3d 1011 (8th Cir. 2006)

**History and docket placement.** Eighth Circuit decision August 22, 2006; OT2006 certiorari and merits review are **hypothetical**. The court affirmed §2 liability and a judicial remedy after South Dakota declined to submit its own plan. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/F3/461/1011/480733/).

**Questions.** Does the proved packing of Native-American voters violate §2 under the lawful alternative and totality, and was a judicial replacement justified after the State declined its opportunity to redraw?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm statutory liability and the bounded remedial plan.

**Reasoning.** Packing Native-American voters can violate §2 when a lawful alternative would preserve an effective district while creating an additional opportunity, and cohesion, bloc voting, and the total circumstances establish unequal opportunity. [*Gingles*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/478/30) supplies the framework; fictional *De Grandy* prevents turning it into mandatory maximization. A high minority percentage is evidence requiring functional explanation, not itself constitutional segregation. The State received the first opportunity to redraw and declined. Fictional *Growe* and *Lopez*, consistent with [*White v. Weiser*, 412 U.S. 783 (1973)](https://www.govinfo.gov/app/details/USREPORTS-412/USREPORTS-412-783/context), permit a necessary cure while preserving lawful state choices.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the alternative’s legality, effective opportunity, cohesion, bloc voting and independent totality; distinguish a statutory remedy from racial maximization. Explain why the concentration here is evidence of the proved statutory injury rather than an automatic constitutional segregation finding. Reserve Segregate and unnecessary constitutional issues. Affirm the bounded remedy after identifying the State’s declined opportunity and the lawful choices the judicial plan preserves.

##### Libertarian Party of Ohio v. Blackwell, 462 F.3d 579 (6th Cir. 2006)

**History and docket placement.** Sixth Circuit decision September 6, 2006; OT2006 Supreme Court merits review is **hypothetical**. The combined early party-petition deadline and mandatory-primary requirement were invalidated; the separate petition-form claim was held moot. [Official opinion](https://www.opn.ca6.uscourts.gov/opinions.pdf/06a0342p-06.pdf).

**Questions.** Do the early petition deadline and mandatory primary, considered together, impose an unjustified ballot-access and associational burden? Does the separate petition-form dispute remain live?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the judgment on the combined qualification rules and the separate mootness disposition.

**Reasoning.** Assess the rules together: a petition due 120 days before a March primary forces party qualification roughly a year before the general election, when new political movements may not yet exist. The State's need to verify petitions does not explain why access must close that early. [*Anderson v. Celebrezze*, 460 U.S. 780 (1983)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/460/780), and fictional *Burdick* provide calibrated review; fictional *Reform Party* reinforces examination of unequal practical routes to participation. This is a direct associational burden, not a districting claim, and requires no Foreclose election history.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the cumulative burden, the actual alternative routes and why the asserted administrative needs do not justify closing access this early. Apply established ballot-access doctrine rather than announce a districting operation. Explain the independent mootness disposition for the form claim. Affirm the invalidation of the combined qualification requirements and the separate mootness judgment, without eliminating lawful verification or election-administration rules.

##### United States v. Osceola County, 475 F. Supp. 2d 1220 (M.D. Fla. 2006)

**History and docket placement.** Single-judge merits decision October 18, 2006; remedial decision December 8, 2006. Retain as an **OT2006 lower-court monitoring entry**, with no invented direct Supreme Court appeal. [Merits opinion](https://openjurist.org/475/fsupp2d/1220/united-states-v-osceola-county-florida-2517543); [remedial opinion](https://openjurist.org/474/fsupp2d/1254/united-states-v-osceola-county-fl-2579712).

**Questions.** What evidence establishes §2 dilution under this at-large system, and how can the district remedy cure unequal opportunity without becoming a proportional-representation mandate?

**Stone’s recommended use.** No Supreme Court action at this stage. Preserve the case as a statutory example supporting relief against proven at-large dilution.

**Reasoning.** [*Gingles*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/478/30) asks whether a compact, cohesive minority can form a workable district and whether majority bloc voting usually defeats its candidates. Fictional *De Grandy* then requires the totality inquiry. An at-large system is neither automatically unlawful nor immune merely because every voter formally casts the same ballot. The proven statutory injury permits a fitted district remedy without a separate constitutional-purpose finding. This case supplies a §2 example, not an Entrench holding and not authority for importing districting thresholds into statutory litigation.

**What the opinion must articulate.** For any use of this lower-court comparator, identify the statutory prerequisites, totality findings and connection between the violation and remedy. Clarify that formal ballot equality does not dispose of §2 and that constitutional racial purpose is not an added element of this results claim. Reserve constitutional and Entrench questions. Record the bounded statutory cure as comparison material; announce no Supreme Court holding and invent no direct appeal.

##### Purcell v. Gonzalez, 549 U.S. 1 (2006)

**History and docket placement.** October 20, 2006: the Court vacated an unexplained appellate injunction against Arizona identification rules shortly before the election, leaving the merits unresolved. Added to explain later emergency-relief decisions. [Opinion](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/549/1/).

**Questions.** Did the court of appeals adequately justify its last-minute injunction, and how should a court weigh disruption against imminent injury from the challenged voting requirements without deciding the underlying merits?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Vacate the appellate injunction and remand for prompt consideration under the proper standard; express no final view on the identification requirements.

**Reasoning.** The appellate court must explain why the district court’s assessment was wrong and weigh both the disruption caused by changing rules and the injury caused by leaving an unlawful restriction in place. Imminence matters to remedy, not to whether a constitutional right exists. The fictional *Burdick* framework governs the underlying burden; [*Reynolds v. Sims*, 377 U.S. 533, 585 (1964)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/377/533/) supplies the earlier analogy for considering election proximity when fashioning relief. Stone should reject a categorical election-season immunity. The same distinction between injury and equitable relief applies to the proposed partisan operations.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the need for reasoned appellate intervention, the applicable review standard and concrete assessment of both election disruption and threatened denial of voting opportunity. Reject categorical election-season immunity and keep the right separate from the timing of relief. Reserve the identification requirements’ ultimate legality. Vacate the unexplained appellate injunction and require prompt consideration under the proper standard, without treating proximity alone as a merits defense.

#### October Term 2007

##### Crawford v. Marion County Election Board — voter identification

**History and docket placement.** Actual Supreme Court merits case, decided April 28, 2008, 553 U.S. 181. The Court affirmed rejection of a facial challenge to Indiana's identification law; the Justices divided over the rationale. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/07-21.ZO.html).

**Questions.** Does the developed record support statewide facial invalidation of Indiana’s identification law when document acquisition, travel, provisional voting and cure are considered together? Which supported applications remain open to challenge?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm rejection of statewide facial invalidation; preserve properly supported challenges to particular applications.

**Reasoning.** Apply the fictional *Burdick* framework to identification, document acquisition, travel, provisional voting, and cure together. Preventing impersonation and maintaining reliable registration are legitimate purposes, but do not erase an actual severe burden. This record does not establish the scope and circumstances needed for the sweeping remedy requested. An indigent voter unable to obtain underlying documents or complete the cure process could present a different case. [*Anderson* (1983)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/460/780/) and [*Burdick* (1992)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/504/428/) supply calibrated burden review; [*Harper* (1966)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/383/663/) explains why a genuine wealth condition would require a different answer. Do not declare every identification requirement valid or presume racial injury from unequal effects alone.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the burden-and-justification analysis and the evidentiary limits on the statewide remedy sought. Identify what the record does and does not establish about affected voters and usable cure procedures. Preserve properly supported challenges involving severe burdens, including genuine wealth barriers, without presuming racial purpose from unequal effects. Affirm rejection of this facial remedy without validating every identification requirement or every application.

#### October Term 2008

##### Bartlett v. Strickland — dependable crossover districts

**History and docket placement.** Actual merits case, March 9, 2009, 556 U.S. 1. The historical Court affirmed North Carolina's judgment, rejecting a §2 obligation to create the proposed district because Black voters were below a numerical majority. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/07-689.ZO.html).

**Questions.** Under existing fictional Valdespino, can dependable crossover support establish the §2 obligation necessary to displace North Carolina’s Whole County Provision despite a Black voting-age share below fifty percent?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Vacate and remand the categorical rejection of the federal defense. Do not immediately approve the map.

**Reasoning.** Fictional *Valdespino* permits a compact district in which a cohesive protected group can elect with dependable crossover support. A 39.36% Black voting-age share therefore does not defeat §2 necessity. Preserve the trial court’s existing crossover and cohesion findings and the stipulated bloc-voting matter; assess any genuinely unresolved issue under the fictional rule rather than order the parties to prove settled matters again. Federal compulsion, not mere permission, is needed to override the state Whole County Provision. [*Gingles* (1986)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/30/) supplies the causal framework; [*De Grandy* (1994)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/512/997/) rejects mechanical ratios and maximization. Their fictional modifications control where actual doctrine differs.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply the existing fictional functional-capacity rule expressly; do not announce numerical exclusivity or treat voluntary permission as federal compulsion. Identify the findings already established, the stipulated matter and any genuinely unresolved statutory elements. Preserve compactness, causation and totality without redoing settled proof. Vacate the categorical rejection of the federal defense and remand only what is necessary to decide whether §2 requires the challenged departure from state law; approve no map automatically.

##### Northwest Austin Municipal Utility District No. One v. Holder — an exit from preclearance

**History and docket placement.** Actual merits case, June 22, 2009, 557 U.S. 193. The Court allowed the district to seek bailout and avoided deciding §5's constitutionality. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supct/html/08-322.ZO.html).

**Questions.** May a covered local governmental unit that does not itself register voters apply for statutory bailout, and must it still satisfy the substantive exit requirements?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reverse and remand for consideration of statutory bailout eligibility on its merits.

**Reasoning.** Congress subjected local governmental units to preclearance even when they did not register voters themselves. The amended bailout provisions should not permanently trap those same units merely because registration occurs elsewhere. [*Sheffield* (1978)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/435/110/) supports the broad coverage context; [*Dougherty County* (1978)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/439/32/) likewise rejects evasion through institutional labels. Eligibility to apply is not automatic entitlement to exit: the statutory requirements remain. This interpretation respects prevention while giving operative effect to Congress's release mechanism. There is no need here to convert fictional *Boerne* into a new Fifteenth Amendment holding or to declare the entire coverage formula unconstitutional.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the eligibility construction in light of coverage and the amended bailout scheme, and distinguish permission to apply from entitlement to relief. Reserve the constitutional validity of §5 and the coverage formula, including any new Fifteenth Amendment enforcement rule. Reverse the exclusion from the bailout process and remand for the actual statutory eligibility and compliance determination.

#### October Term 2009

##### League of Women Voters of Ohio v. Brunner — systemic failures in access

**History and docket placement.** The Sixth Circuit’s November 26, 2008 decision, 548 F.3d 463, allowed equal-protection and substantive-due-process claims but rejected the pleaded procedural theory. The remaining claims settled June 19, 2009. Retain this as an OT2009 comparison; a merits slot requires an explicit counterfactual that the dispute remained live. [Opinion](https://app.midpage.ai/document/league-of-women-voters-of-2977261); [counsel’s settlement history](https://www.lawyerscommittee.org/project/league-of-women-voters-v-brunner/).

**Questions.** Do alleged severe and recurring election-administration failures state equal-protection or substantive-due-process claims against responsible officials? Does the pleaded procedural theory identify a separate deprivation, and is any controversy still live?

**Stone’s recommended use.** No merits action on the settled real record. If a live appeal is expressly reconstructed, affirm the appellate disposition and remand without finding the allegations proved.

**Reasoning.** Allegations of severe, recurring shortages and arbitrary treatment can state an injury to the franchise when connected to responsible officials and actual voters. A constitutional system need not be administratively identical everywhere, but it cannot materially deny access through arbitrary allocation. [*Reynolds* (1964)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/377/533/) supplies equal political participation; fictional *Burdick* supplies burden review. The alleged deprivation is the voting opportunity itself, not denial of a particular hearing, so the procedural theory adds no demonstrated claim. Racial *Burden* would require its own purpose or classification allegations; uneven resources alone do not supply them.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Keep the settled actual case as comparison material. Only in an expressly reconstructed live appeal should the opinion clarify the difference between actionable denial of voting opportunity, ordinary local variation and a separate procedural deprivation. Identify personal exposure, official responsibility and the fundamental-unfairness requirement; allegations are not findings. Reserve racial Burden absent its own required allegations. The conditional remedy is further proceedings on the surviving claims, not a declaration that the alleged system-wide violations are proved.

##### United States v. Brown — protection does not depend on the voter's race

**History and docket placement.** Proposed certiorari review in OT2009 of 561 F.3d 420 (5th Cir. 2009), including the revised opinion filed April 1, 2009; no actual Supreme Court merits disposition is assumed. The Fifth Circuit sustained liability for intentional discrimination against white voters in Noxubee County Democratic primaries. [Appellate opinion](https://docs.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/ca5/07-60588/920090401).

**Questions.** Does §2 prohibit the proved intentional discrimination against white voters in party-administered public primaries, and which practices may be enjoined to cure it?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the §2 judgment and appropriately confined relief.

**Reasoning.** Section 2 protects any citizen against denial or abridgment on account of race. Selective challenges, manipulation of absentee procedures, and unequal administration cannot be justified by the race of their beneficiaries. The findings, rather than a general racial disparity, establish the discrimination. [*United States v. Classic* (1941)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/313/299/) and [*Smith v. Allwright* (1944)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/321/649/) explain why a public electoral function cannot escape federal protection merely because a party administers it. The fictional *Brooks* purpose analysis supplies a consistent method for assessing causation. Remedy the discriminatory practices, not the racial composition of elected officials.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify that §2’s protection applies to voters of every race and that a public primary function does not escape federal voting protections through party administration. Tie intent and causation to the actual findings and statutory elements; do not infer discrimination merely from racial disparities. Reserve any entitlement to a particular racial composition of officials. Affirm the judgment and confine relief to the discriminatory challenge, absentee and administration practices proved.

#### October Term 2010

**No case is assigned to this term.** Existing statutory and constitutional rules continue to govern lower courts; this gap announces no new holding.

#### October Term 2011

##### Perry v. Perez — lawful interim maps

**History and docket placement.** Actual Supreme Court decision, January 20, 2012, 565 U.S. 388. The Court vacated the Texas interim-map orders and remanded because the applicable deference rules were unclear in the lower court's work. [Opinion](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/565/388/).

**Questions.** What deference must an interim-map court give an enacted plan challenged under the Constitution, §2, and an unresolved §5 preclearance process, and which legal defects justify departure from state policy?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Vacate and remand for maps applying the correct interim standards.

**Reasoning.** An interim court does not acquire a general commission to choose its favorite map. It should preserve lawful state policy choices, depart from provisions likely to violate the Constitution or §2, and avoid using changes presenting a reasonable probability of failing preclearance. The Texas court must not itself grant preclearance entrusted to the statutory process. [*White v. Weiser* (1973)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/412/783/) and [*Upham v. Seamon* (1982)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/456/37/) support respecting lawful legislative choices; fictional *Lopez* preserves the prohibition against implementing unprecleared changes. The governing fictional territorial doctrine also prevents race-conscious district design alone from becoming a reason to discard the State's policy.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the different interim standards for likely constitutional or §2 defects and the reasonable probability of failure under §5; identify the state policies the court must preserve and the changes it must avoid. Apply existing fictional territorial and preclearance holdings rather than creating a design-only constitutional claim. Remand for corrected interim maps without the reviewing court itself granting preclearance or prescribing an ideal plan.

##### Fletcher v. Lamone — prisoner residence and the limits of a party's seat tally

**History and docket placement.** Three-judge decision December 23, 2011, 831 F. Supp. 2d 887; actually summarily affirmed June 25, 2012. [District opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/maryland/mddce/8:2011cv03220/195770/54/).

**Questions.** May Maryland assign prisoners to supported home addresses for redistricting, and did these plaintiffs establish a preserved racial or partisan electoral injury from the resulting congressional plan?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm on the claims actually pursued. Plaintiffs did not establish their racial claims and did not pursue an independent partisan theory untethered to racial animus. The proposed partisan rule would not impose an additional completed-election requirement. This judgment does not decide absent Sixth District voters’ claims.

**Reasoning.** A supported residence adjustment may better locate represented populations; it is not discriminatory merely because it changes racial totals. [*Burns v. Richardson* (1966)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/384/73/) illustrates the permissible attention to population bases, subject to equal representation. The §2 claim also failed compactness and bloc-voting proof, independently of any numerical floor. A statewide Republican seat loss does not establish these plaintiffs' injury. Under the proposed partisan rule, a properly preserved concrete threatened injury need not await an election. Material interference with actual association or concrete efforts to organize or participate has no separate duration prerequisite. Fictional *Miller/Sinkfield* require personal injury; [*Davis v. Bandemer* (1986)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/109/) supports justiciability while supplying no proportional-seat entitlement. Judge Titus's concurrence expressly records that plaintiffs did not pursue the independent partisan theory; his criticism of the map cannot supply their missing claim.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Specify the supported residence rule and each independent failure of the racial claims. Identify the absence of a pursued independent partisan claim as a preservation limit, not a substantive bar to pre-election injury. Reserve absent Sixth District voters’ claims and any unpresented association theory. Affirm only the judgment supported by this record; do not announce a seat-allocation entitlement or a new partisan merits rule unnecessarily.

##### Committee for a Fair and Balanced Map v. Illinois State Board of Elections — separate the three claims

**History and docket placement.** Proposed direct merits review of the three-judge decision of December 15, 2011, 835 F. Supp. 2d 563. The actual court rejected statutory, racial-intent, racial-design, and partisan challenges. [Opinion](https://openjurist.org/835/fsupp2d/563/committee-for-a-fair-balanced-map-v-illinois-state-board-of-elections).

**Questions.** May a §2 claim be rejected solely because the proposed Latino district is below a numerical majority; did the evidence establish prohibited racial purpose; and can the preserved partisan claim proceed under a bounded judicial standard before an election?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm rejection of racial design alone and the supported no-racial-purpose finding; vacate the categorical §2 numerical-majority ruling. Also vacate the partisan no-standard dismissal and remand preserved Foreclose claims if the proposed *Vieth* holding has entered simulated law; otherwise expressly adopt the necessary justiciability and injury rule before remanding. Those claims may proceed before an election; Entrench remains unavailable for a congressional delegation.

**Reasoning.** The actual opinion rejected a proposed Latino district at 46.5% voting-age population under historical *Bartlett*. Fictional *Valdespino* forbids that categorical barrier: dependable crossover support must be considered, with all other statutory requirements intact.
