Political purpose does not establish racial purpose or excuse a separately proved partisan injury. The proposed Foreclose rule requires either complete loss of effective electoral opportunity in every destination throughout the recorded conditions over the prior lawful map’s life, or material impairment of actual association or concrete efforts to organize or participate, with prohibited purpose and causation. An emerging electorate may use an independently grounded lawful comparison without prior electoral success; no completed-election gate applies. [*Arlington Heights* (1977)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/429/252/) supplies the intent inquiry; [*Gingles*](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/30/) supplies statutory elements subject to *Valdespino*.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply fictional *Valdespino* to remove numerical exclusivity while retaining compactness, cohesion, relevant bloc-voting causation, and totality. Preserve supported racial-purpose findings. For any partisan remand, state whether the necessary Foreclose rule is existing simulated law or a new holding; define its two independent injuries, personal standing, prohibited purpose, causation, and the same-decision defense. Require independently grounded lawful comparisons, allow supported pre-election and emerging-electorate proof, and exclude congressional Entrench claims. No liability or mandatory redraw follows from remand alone.

##### Radogno v. Illinois State Board of Elections — plead an injury, not just political advantage

**History and docket placement.** Proposed review after final disposition, including the November 22, 2011 partisan opinion and December 7, 2011 final summary judgment, 836 F. Supp. 2d 759. The November opinion dismissed partisan counts with prejudice for failure to supply a workable standard. [Partisan opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/illinois/ilndce/1:2011cv04884/257964/84/).

**Questions.** Should partisan counts be dismissed with prejudice for lack of a workable standard, or may plaintiffs plead and prove a concrete representation, association, or whole-chamber control injury under a defined rule?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Vacate the partisan dismissal with prejudice and remand under the proposed *Vieth* rule if adopted, allowing conforming pleadings; otherwise expressly decide the necessary new rule before applying it. Neither Foreclose nor Entrench requires completed elections under the challenged map. Independently resolved racial or state-law counts are not automatically reopened.

**Reasoning.** Democratic control, irregular lines, and an incumbent’s inconvenience do not establish the proposed wrongs. Foreclose requires either complete representation loss in every destination throughout the recorded conditions over the prior lawful map’s life, or material impairment of actual association or concrete organizing or participation efforts. The branches remain independent. Entrench requires reliable evidence of retained control throughout recorded opposition-majority conditions, considered for the whole chamber and connected to the map rather than geography alone. Supported inference can establish threatened injury before voting; bare predictions cannot. [*Baker v. Carr* (1962)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/369/186/) and [*Bandemer* (1986)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/109/) support judicial competence without establishing Stone’s exact proposed standard. The remand concerns proper pleading and proof, not a declaration that Illinois’s map is unlawful.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Explain why conforming allegations may proceed under a defined rule without declaring liability. Distinguish representation Foreclose, association Foreclose, and whole-chamber Entrench; announce a necessary new rule only if earlier simulated decisions have not adopted it. Specify the recorded-range inquiry, independently grounded lawful comparisons and emerging-electorate route, without prescribing an election count or statistical method. Association requires material interference with actual activity or concrete efforts, without a duration gate. Preserve ordinary amendment rules and independent judgments on other counts.

##### Baldus v. Members of the Wisconsin Government Accountability Board — a confined §2 remedy

**History and docket placement.** Proposed direct merits review of the three-judge decision of March 22, 2012, 849 F. Supp. 2d 840, and the April 11 remedial order. The court found a §2 violation in Assembly Districts 8 and 9 and rejected the remaining claims. [Liability opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/wisconsin/wiedce/2:2011cv00562/56481/210/); [remedy](https://docs.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/wisconsin/wiedce/2:2011cv00562/56481/238).

**Questions.** Did the Wisconsin Assembly configuration deny Latino voters equal electoral opportunity under §2, and may relief be confined to the districts whose boundaries caused the established violation?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the established §2 violation and the confined correction of Districts 8 and 9.

**Reasoning.** The identified Latino community, voting patterns, and lawful remedial configuration support a statutory result without a separate constitutional finding of discriminatory purpose. [*Gingles*](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/478/30/) supplies the causal electoral inquiry; [*De Grandy*](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/512/997/) requires totality rather than maximizing districts. After a reasonable state opportunity to cure, [*Upham*](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/456/37/) supports correcting the affected districts while preserving lawful parts of the plan. This does not adjudicate the later statewide partisan-entrenchment record in *Gill*. The overlap of maps does not make distinct claims or later facts identical.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Identify the supported statutory elements and why Districts 8 and 9 supply a lawful, workable correction. Keep statutory results separate from constitutional purpose, and distinguish opportunity from racial seat maximization. Explain the State’s opportunity to cure and the remedial necessity of the confined changes. Affirm that correction without deciding a later statewide partisan record or reopening otherwise lawful districts.

#### October Term 2012

##### Shelby County v. Holder — retain preventive enforcement with a substantive connection

**History and docket placement.** Actual merits case, June 25, 2013, 570 U.S. 529. The historical Court invalidated §4(b)'s coverage formula; it did not formally invalidate §5 itself. [Opinion and dissent](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/570/529/).

**Questions.** Does the renewed coverage formula remain a permissible preventive exercise of Fifteenth Amendment enforcement power on the contemporary congressional record, and what relationship does that source-specific inquiry bear to fictional *Boerne*?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm rejection of the facial challenge, retaining the coverage formula and preclearance. Apply the source-specific Fifteenth Amendment precedents; do not imply that the completed fictional *Boerne* decision already resolved their exact relationship to its Fourteenth Amendment standard.

**Reasoning.** Apply [*South Carolina v. Katzenbach* (1966)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/383/301/) and [*City of Rome* (1980)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/446/156/) as the source-specific Fifteenth Amendment precedents preserved by fictional *Boerne*. Read their preventive authority consistently with *Boerne*: Congress may intercept recurring racial obstruction, but may not redefine the constitutional right. The renewal record of continuing discrimination, shifting practices, and the inadequacy of case-by-case relief supplies the substantive connection; bailout allows jurisdictions meeting Congress's conditions to leave. Improved participation under an operative remedy does not alone establish that the remedy is unnecessary. Historic trigger dates deserve scrutiny but are not an automatic expiration rule. This is a present-fit judgment, not permanent approval of every renewal or every application.

**What the opinion must articulate.** State expressly that the opinion applies the source-specific Fifteenth Amendment precedents preserved, but not comprehensively reconciled, by fictional *Boerne*. Explain the constitutional injuries Congress may prevent, the continuing record connection, and the operation of bailout. Do not redefine the underlying right or approve coverage permanently. Affirm the facial rejection while leaving a properly presented later application or renewal challenge to its own record; broader extension of *Boerne* is not silently adopted.

##### Texas v. United States — apply the surviving preclearance rule

**History and docket placement.** D.D.C. denied preclearance August 28, 2012, 887 F. Supp. 2d 133. Texas adopted replacement plans June 23, 2013, effective June 26; the Supreme Court’s June 27 remand referenced both *Shelby County* and the suggestion of mootness. The district court had found failures to establish nonretrogression for the congressional and House plans and absence of discriminatory purpose for the congressional and Senate plans. [Case record](https://clearinghouse.net/case/12380/); [later opinion documenting the procedural sequence](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/cadc/14-5151/14-5151-2015-08-18.html).

**Questions.** Does replacement legislation moot review of denied preclearance; if a live controversy remains, did Texas satisfy the separate nonretrogression and discriminatory-purpose requirements for the challenged plans?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Hold for *Shelby County*, then remand to determine the effect of the actual replacement legislation on mootness and appropriate disposition. If a live preclearance issue remains, sustain the denial on supported findings under the surviving statute; do not issue an advisory merits affirmance of superseded plans.

**Reasoning.** The submitting jurisdiction bears the statutory burden; a claimed political explanation does not erase findings of racial dilution. Fictional *Bossier I/II* independently bar dilutive purpose, even without intended retrogression. [*Beer* (1976)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/425/130/) supplies the historical nonretrogression concept, while the 2006 statutory text governs this later record. The ruling withholds permission for these changes; it does not select a replacement map or treat every demographic decline as lost opportunity.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Resolve mootness and the proper treatment of the judgment before reaching merits. If a live controversy survives, identify which plan fails which statutory requirement and apply the submitting jurisdiction’s burden under the 2006 text and fictional *Bossier*. Do not transplant a constitutional same-decision defense into §5 automatically. Explain the sequence after the simulated *Shelby County* decision and the limits of denying permission to implement a change; no advisory affirmance or judicial replacement map.

##### Obama for America v. Husted — unequal early-voting deadlines

**History and docket placement.** Proposed expedited review of the Sixth Circuit's October 5, 2012 decision, 697 F.3d 423, affirming preliminary relief for the November election; this is a hypothetical merits treatment, not an actual Supreme Court merits opinion. [Appellate opinion](https://docs.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/ca6/12-4055/6111457654).

**Questions.** Did Ohio adequately justify withdrawing the final three days of civilian early voting while retaining them for military voters, and was preliminary restoration of access warranted on the election record?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the preliminary injunction restoring access on equal terms, without curtailing military voters' existing rights.

**Reasoning.** Military needs may justify additional opportunities. They do not, by themselves, explain removing an established civilian opportunity during the same final three days. Under fictional *Burdick*, weigh the actual burden and the administrative reasons; Ohio's asserted administrative concerns lacked a sufficient connection to the challenged distinction on this record. [*Anderson*](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/460/780/) supplies the balancing inquiry, and fictional *Reform Party* supports requiring a justification for unequal access inside one electoral system. This establishes no universal constitutional entitlement to a particular number of early-voting days and no final liability beyond the preliminary record.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply the election-access balancing rule to the actual unequal deadline, the evidence of burden, the State’s justification, and the preliminary-injunction requirements. Preserve justified additional military protections. Clarify that equal access on this record creates neither a fixed constitutional early-voting calendar nor a final merits finding beyond the preliminary record. State the immediate administrative scope of the injunction.

#### October Term 2013

##### Green Party of Tennessee v. Hargett — evaluate the rules actually in force

**History and docket placement.** Proposed certiorari review of the Sixth Circuit's November 30, 2012 decision, 700 F.3d 816, following statutory changes during appeal. The court reversed the district judgment and remanded for renewed consideration. [Opinion](https://openjurist.org/700/f3d/816/green-party-v-hargett).

**Questions.** How do statutory amendments change the combined nomination and ballot-access burdens, and do continuing party disparities or the actual ballot-order system impose an unjustified burden?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the remand, expressly preserving scrutiny of continuing party disparities and concrete ballot-order burdens.

**Reasoning.** A changed deadline and altered nomination route can change the combined burden, so constitutional analysis must address the operative system. [*Anderson*](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/460/780/) and [*Norman v. Reed* (1992)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/502/279/) protect meaningful access while permitting reasonable demonstrations of support. Fictional *Reform Party* requires justification for unequal treatment of qualifying parties. An incumbent-party preference is not immune simply because it appears as ballot administration. Conversely, a statistical claim about positional advantage must connect to the ballot format actually used; studies of a different design do not establish this injury. The remand is neither blanket approval of amended law nor a finding of racial or partisan districting injury.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Identify the amended provisions and the claims that remain live. Require an assessment of cumulative operative burdens, continuing party inequalities, and evidence tied to the ballot design actually used. Clarify what ordinary support requirements remain permissible and which justifications require reconsideration. Affirm a focused remand without validating the entire amended scheme or importing a districting-specific injury threshold.

##### Chamness v. Bowen / Chamness v. Maldonado — an accurate ballot description

**History and docket placement.** Proposed certiorari review of the consolidated Ninth Circuit decision of July 3, 2013, 722 F.3d 1110. The appellate caption includes *Chamness v. Maldonado*; *Chamness v. Bowen* identifies the Secretary-of-State dispute. The court upheld the challenged label and resolved separate mootness/intervention matters. [Opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/appellate-courts/ca9/11-56303/11-56303-2013-07-03.html).

**Questions.** Does the challenged party-preference label impose a constitutionally significant burden or discriminatory official designation, and do the separately decided mootness and intervention issues warrant disturbance?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the rejection of Chamness's label challenge; leave independently justified mootness and intervention dispositions intact.

**Reasoning.** A neutral indication that no qualified party preference is recorded does not expel a candidate from political participation or impose a pejorative status. Chamness retained a route to the ballot and could communicate his political identity outside the official label. Apply fictional *Burdick*, with [*Timmons* (1997)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/520/351/) as a ballot-regulation comparison. Distinguish [*Cook v. Gralike* (2001)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/531/510/), which concerned official ballot labels designed to punish candidates over policy positions. The distinction depends on the label's function, not a rule that official words can never burden political association.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Explain why the actual label is an informational description rather than punishment for political identity or policy positions, applying the relevant burden and justification inquiry. Distinguish *Cook* without categorically insulating official ballot language. Identify the independently justified mootness and intervention rulings left intact. Affirm the label judgment without recognizing a general governmental power to stigmatize candidates or suppress association.

#### October Term 2014

##### Alabama Legislative Black Caucus v. Alabama — 575 U.S. 254 (March 25, 2015)

**History and docket placement.** The actual Court vacated and remanded the rejection of racial-gerrymandering claims. Alabama sought to maintain existing Black population percentages in majority-Black districts. The actual opinion expressly left additional population-equality and vote-dilution claims unresolved. In this timeline, retain a **reconstructed appeal on those independent claims**, rather than importing a racial-predominance cause of action. [Actual opinion, especially Part V](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/13-895).

**Questions.** Which district-specific, preserved population-equality, intentional-dilution, or participation claims survive rejection of territorial racial design as an independent injury, and did an erroneous standard prevent their adjudication?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reject design-only liability; vacate and remand any preserved intentional-dilution or concrete participation claim that was rejected under an inconsistent standard. Identify the affected districts and voters before ordering relief.

**Reasoning.** Section 5 protects effective opportunity, not an immutable racial percentage. Packing can impair surrounding communities, but the claimant must identify the lost electoral opportunity or concrete participation injury and its causal connection to prohibited racial treatment. A compliance explanation is evidence, not immunity; [*Arlington Heights*, 429 U.S. 252 (1977)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/252), supports examining its sincerity and role. Fictional *Shaw–Miller–Vera* bars design-only liability. The proposed racial Burden rule covers actual association, realistic coalition-building, and concrete participation efforts without requiring a lost winnable district or a separate duration threshold. The proposed Dilute rule protects materially impaired electoral opportunity without fixed election counts. Adopt only the rule necessary for a preserved claim, rather than treating either proposal as completed precedent. Reserve **Segregate** in this docket. Numerical targets alone do not establish its distinct separative purpose or operation.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Identify the preserved independent claims, affected voters and districts, and the specific erroneous standard warranting any remand. Apply existing rejection of design-only liability; expressly announce any necessary development of racial participation or material dilution protection if not already adopted. Coalition and participation injury need not include a lost winnable district, and no fixed election or duration requirement is imposed. Examine compliance purpose and causation without granting immunity. Reserve Segregate and prescribe relief only after a proved independent violation.

##### Arizona State Legislature v. Arizona Independent Redistricting Commission — 576 U.S. 787 (June 29, 2015)

**History and docket placement.** The actual Court upheld use of the commission for congressional districting. Retain the historical appeal and OT placement. [Actual opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/13-1314).

**Questions.** Does the Elections Clause permit congressional districting by an independent commission created through Arizona’s initiative lawmaking process?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the commission's authority and reject the Legislature's Elections Clause challenge.

**Reasoning.** Congressional redistricting is an exercise of state lawmaking, and the Elections Clause ordinarily works through the State's constitutionally established legislative process. [*Ohio ex rel. Davis v. Hildebrant*, 241 U.S. 565 (1916)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/241/565), accepted the referendum as part of that process; [*Smiley v. Holm*, 285 U.S. 355 (1932)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/285/355), required compliance with gubernatorial veto rules. Extending that reasoning to Arizona's initiative-created commission respects state lawmaking arrangements and the language of 2 U.S.C. §2a(c). Commission independence supplies no immunity from a later constitutional or VRA claim: the authority to draw districts and the lawfulness of the districts drawn are separate questions.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Resolve the meaning of the State’s legislative process under the Elections Clause and explain the extension from referendum and veto precedents to the initiative-created commission. Address §2a(c) as statutory support without treating it as a general immunity provision. Affirm the commission’s authority; reserve independent challenges to the maps it produces under the Constitution and voting statutes.

#### October Term 2015

##### Shapiro v. McManus — 577 U.S. 39 (December 8, 2015)

**History and docket placement.** The actual Court reversed the Fourth Circuit: a single district judge could not refuse the requested three-judge court merely because the constitutional complaint appeared to fail on the merits. Retain the historical case. [Actual opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/14-990).

**Questions.** May a single judge dismiss this constitutional apportionment action as legally insufficient without convening the three-judge court required by 28 U.S.C. §2284?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reverse and remand for a three-judge district court. Do **not** announce a completed Foreclose violation or treat this procedural judgment as a ruling that every substantive element was adequately pleaded.

**Reasoning.** Congress made the three-judge procedure mandatory for the covered constitutional apportionment action. [*Goosby v. Osser*, 409 U.S. 512 (1973)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/409/512), distinguishes a wholly insubstantial federal claim from a claim that may ultimately fail. Maryland voters' political-association theory crosses the jurisdictional threshold, especially if the simulated *Vieth* has already recognized a bounded partisan claim. The properly constituted court must then decide standing, pleading, proof, and any remedy. That division respects both the statutory procedure and Stone's insistence that identifying an arguable injury is not proving it.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Separate statutory three-judge entitlement and substantial federal jurisdiction from pleading sufficiency and ultimate liability. Clarify the single judge’s limited authority and order constitution of the proper court. If a proposed earlier partisan holding is invoked, identify its actual adoption status. Reserve standing, all contested merits elements, and relief; no new Foreclose holding or completed violation is needed for this procedural judgment.

##### Evenwel v. Abbott, 578 U.S. 54 (2016)

**History and docket placement.** April 4, 2016: the Court affirmed dismissal of the demand that Texas equalize eligible voters rather than total population in state senate districts. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/14-940).

**Questions.** Does equal protection require Texas to equalize eligible voters instead of total residents when drawing state legislative districts?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm. Texas may use total resident population for legislative representation.

**Reasoning.** Representatives serve children, noncitizens, and other residents as well as voters; equalizing their constituencies serves a constitutionally legitimate conception of representation. This follows the completed fictional *Chen* holding and the representational reasoning of [*Reynolds v. Sims*, 377 U.S. 533 (1964)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/377/533/). [*Burns v. Richardson*, 384 U.S. 73 (1966)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/384/73/) does not turn a permitted alternative in its particular circumstances into a universal voter-population command. Reserve the validity of other denominators. The appropriate measure for a VRA opportunity-to-elect claim remains a separate question.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply fictional *Chen* and explain the representational basis for permitting total resident population. Distinguish residents’ representation from the electorate measure relevant to a particular VRA claim. Affirm Texas’s choice without holding that every alternative denominator is lawful or that equal eligible-voter population is constitutionally mandatory.

#### October Term 2016

##### Bethune-Hill v. Virginia State Board of Elections — 580 U.S. 178 (March 1, 2017)

**History and docket placement.** The district court rejected all twelve challenges. The actual Supreme Court affirmed as to District 75 and vacated and remanded as to eleven districts. A 55% Black voting-age-population target framed the litigation. Preserve this as a **counterfactual review only if an independent issue survives** the fictional rejection of *Shaw*-style liability. [Actual opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/15-680).

**Questions.** Does the 55% racial target establish a constitutional injury under entering fictional law, and does any independently preserved claim justify a remand apart from the rejected design theory?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm rejection of the design-only claims on the fictional *Shaw–Miller–Vera* ground. A remand requires an independently preserved claim and a specified error in its treatment; it is not an automatic invitation to invent a dilution case after trial.

**Reasoning.** A numerical racial target may reveal the mapmaker's method without establishing exclusion, unequal participation, or intentional loss of electoral opportunity. Conversely, preservation of the ability to elect in one district does not excuse a proved impairment elsewhere. [*Whitcomb v. Chavis*, 403 U.S. 124 (1971)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/403/124), supplies the caution against deriving deprivation from political outcomes alone. Where an independent purposeful-injury claim exists, [*Arlington Heights*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/252) supplies the ordinary intent inquiry. The record does not justify adopting **Segregate** simply because several districts shared the same target.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Affirm the design-only rejection under entering fictional law, explaining why common racial targets are evidence rather than an independent deprivation. Identify any actual preserved claim and specified legal error before granting a remand; do not manufacture a new cause after trial. Reserve Segregate and questions requiring absent facts. A protected opportunity in one district does not resolve another claimant’s proved injury.

##### Cooper v. Harris — 581 U.S. 285 (May 22, 2017)

**History and docket placement.** The actual Court affirmed judgments invalidating both districts as racial gerrymanders. Both districts had elected Black voters' preferred candidates before becoming majority-Black. The historical action depends on doctrine rejected in this simulation; label any retained independent-claim review **reconstructed**. [Actual opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/15-1262).

**Questions.** Can relief based solely on racial predominance survive fictional precedent, and does a preserved independent claim establish material racial impairment of electoral opportunity or participation?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reverse relief resting solely on racial predominance. Remand only independently preserved intentional-dilution or concrete participation claims requiring decision under the correct rule.

**Reasoning.** Excess concentration can materially impair an actual association or surrounding voters’ participation without extinguishing the packed district’s electoral success. The proposed coalition form of racial Burden requires its own pleaded mechanism, material injury to actual association or realistic organizing and participation efforts, purpose or unequal treatment, and causation; Dilute separately requires material opportunity impairment. Neither requires a separately prescribed duration or number of completed elections. Neither imports Foreclose’s complete-loss threshold. Under fictional *Easley*, a partisan explanation is neither a complete defense nor proof of racial intent. [*Arlington Heights*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/252) permits direct and circumstantial proof without a universal alternative-map requirement. Preserve supported historical findings while rejecting predominance-to-strict-scrutiny reasoning. Later use of the 2016 remedial map must separately explain its existence in this timeline.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Reverse the design-only relief while specifying which factual findings remain usable and which independent claims, if any, were preserved. If a new racial participation or dilution rule is necessary, announce its scope expressly and keep it distinct from complete representation Foreclose. Require the actual impairment, prohibited treatment, and causation; avoid a mandatory proof method or election wait. Limit remand and remedy to those claims and explain the counterfactual status of any later remedial map.

#### October Term 2017

##### Gill v. Whitford — 585 U.S. 48 (June 18, 2018)

**History and docket placement.** The actual Court vacated and remanded for further proceedings on standing, without deciding the partisan-gerrymandering merits. Retain this procedural vehicle. [Actual opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/16-1161).

**Questions.** Have these plaintiffs established personal injury and redressability for the claims they actually presented, and can any preserved associational or whole-chamber theory proceed without converting a statewide seat preference into standing?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Vacate and remand for plaintiffs to establish their own injury and redressability, or a properly preserved independent associational theory. Standing precedes merits, and the remand does not create a new count. A properly presented claim under the proposed Entrench rule requires no fixed number of completed elections.

**Reasoning.** A preferred statewide seat distribution is not itself personal injury. Fictional *Miller* and *Sinkfield* require a connection to the challenged governmental treatment. For a properly presented Entrench theory, test control throughout reliably measured opposition-majority conditions over the recorded range, whole-body coverage, and map causation; one inversion or a seat ratio alone does not establish those elements. [*Reynolds v. Sims*, 377 U.S. 533 (1964)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/377/533), supports effective representation without prescribing partisan seat entitlement; [*Gaffney*, 412 U.S. 735 (1973)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/412/735), preserves lawful political considerations. November 2018 results are future evidence as of this June decision, not part of its record.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Resolve standing and redressability claim by claim without deciding unpresented merits or creating an association count. Distinguish an individual injury from dissatisfaction with statewide seat totals. If a preserved Entrench theory requires clarification, identify whole-chamber coverage, recorded opposition-majority conditions, and map causation; no two-cycle or other fixed election gate applies. Treat independently grounded lawful comparisons and supported pre-election evidence according to their relevance. Do not import November 2018 results into the June record.

##### Benisek v. Lamone — 585 U.S. 155 (June 18, 2018)

**History and docket placement.** The actual Court affirmed denial of a preliminary injunction against the 2018 election under the challenged plan. Retain this interlocutory appeal. [Actual opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/17-333).

**Questions.** Did delay, the approaching election timetable, and the balance of equities justify denying preliminary relief while the constitutional challenge proceeded to a merits decision?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the denial on the demonstrated delay, election-administration timetable, and balance of equities; direct prompt merits proceedings. Do not affirm merely because the real Court was awaiting clarification from *Gill* if fictional precedent already supplies the substantive rule.

**Reasoning.** A substantial constitutional claim does not automatically justify a late change to election arrangements. [*Reynolds v. Sims*, 377 U.S. 533, 585 (1964)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/377/533), recognizes that timing and an election already underway can affect equitable relief. Here the plaintiffs sought preliminary relief in May 2017, years after enactment, and identified an August deadline for an orderly redraw. The court can protect administration for the approaching election while preserving a judgment and timely remedy for the next one. This is a remedial timing decision, not a holding that the challenged map is constitutional or that an election must occur before suit can ever be filed.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Specify the preliminary-relief factors and record-supported timing reasons for affirmance, while separating election administration from constitutional liability. Direct prompt merits consideration and preserve a practicable prospective remedy if liability follows. Neither delay in seeking this injunction nor a completed election becomes a substantive prerequisite to suit. Do not rely on doctrinal uncertainty that an already adopted simulated holding has resolved.

##### Abbott v. Perez, 585 U.S. 579 (2018)

**History and docket placement.** June 25, 2018: the Court reversed the challenged intent and §2 rulings but sustained the racial-design ruling against House District 90. This appeal presents the effect of reenactment on the constitutional inquiry. [Opinion and dissent](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/17-586).

**Questions.** Which findings establish discriminatory purpose in the operative enactment, what burden follows a motivating-purpose showing, and which independent §2 claims or design-only remedies survive that analysis?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reverse relief resting solely on racial design, including HD90 and the design theory concerning CD35. Vacate the challenged intent judgments for application of the operative-enactment and motivating-factor sequence, preserving supported subsidiary findings. Remand the separate §2 results claims concerning CD27 and HD32/HD34 for the fictional opportunity standard; do not erase them merely because an intent finding fails.

**Reasoning.** Under fictional *Brooks*, earlier discrimination is relevant evidence, but liability must attach to the operative enactment. Copying an interim judicial map neither automatically cleanses discriminatory purpose nor proves it. The claimant must establish prohibited motivating purpose; only then does the State bear the distinct burden of proving the same enactment would have occurred without it. [*Arlington Heights*, 429 U.S. 252 (1977)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/429/252/), and [*Hunter v. Underwood*, 471 U.S. 222 (1985)](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/471/222/), support examining continuity, present choices, and causation rather than demanding ritual proof of legislative repentance. Review properly made intent findings for clear error. Independently, fictional *Valdespino* allows dependable crossover opportunities and *De Grandy* rejects conclusive statewide offsets; neither guarantees plaintiffs an additional district. Reject racial predominance alone under fictional *Shaw/Miller/Vera*. If the proposed *Shelby County* disposition preserves coverage, the actual post-*Shelby* clearance history must also be reconstructed rather than imported unchanged.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Distinguish each design-only, operative-purpose, and statutory-results judgment component. State the motivating-factor and same-enactment sequence, allocate its burdens, and preserve supported findings under clear-error review. Apply fictional crossover and totality rules to the independent §2 remand without guaranteeing an additional district. Identify the procedural and preclearance reconstruction required if simulated *Shelby County* preserves coverage. Any remand must identify actual error rather than require a ritual declaration of legislative repentance.

#### October Term 2018

##### Lamone v. Benisek — No. 18-726 (June 27, 2019)

**History and docket placement.** The actual Court vacated the Maryland judgment together with *Rucho*, treating federal partisan-gerrymandering claims as nonjusticiable. Retain the merits appeal, distinct from the 2018 preliminary-relief case. [Actual Supreme Court disposition](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/18-422).

**Questions.** Does purposeful districting materially impair identified voters’ actual association or organizing and participation efforts on this record, and what prospective remedy addresses that injury independently of complete representation foreclosure?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm liability on the independently supported association branch and prospective relief for the next practicable election, with the State first to draw a lawful replacement. Apply that rule if already adopted in the simulation; otherwise announce the necessary new holding expressly. Identify the concrete organizational findings supporting this ground. Do not treat affirmance as a finding of representation foreclosure throughout the recorded range; that distinct theory needs its own findings if necessary to disposition.

**Reasoning.** The record goes beyond Bartlett’s defeat and a change in party totals. It identifies functioning local Republican organizations, district-fragmentation confusion that diverted organizing work, and impaired participation, volunteer activity, and fundraising, connected to the targeted reassignment. Explain why the supported findings show material impairment of actual political activity; persistence reinforces the evidence but is not a separate prerequisite, and subjective discouragement or an unfavorable election alone is insufficient. The net shift of roughly 66,000 registered Republicans out and 24,000 Democrats in, compared with a population-equality need to remove 10,186 residents, supports purpose and causation but is not itself injury. [District opinion, Parts I and IV](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/maryland/mddce/1:2013cv03233/258247/222/). [*Elrod*, 427 U.S. 347 (1976)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/427/347), supplies the political-retaliation analogy; [*Mt. Healthy*, 429 U.S. 274 (1977)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/274), supplies the causal safeguard. This extends those principles to districting rather than claiming they already decided it. Relief cures the organizational injury without guaranteeing a Republican seat.

**What the opinion must articulate.** State whether the association rule is newly adopted or an application of an already adopted simulated holding; *Elrod* and *Mt. Healthy* are analogies, not prior districting decisions. Identify the record findings of concrete material organizational impairment, prohibited one-sided purpose, causation, and failure of any same-decision defense. Actual activity and concrete efforts to organize or participate are protected without a duration or election-count gate. Reserve representation foreclosure if unnecessary, and explain why the prospective, State-first remedy cures association injury without promising a Republican seat.

##### Rucho v. Common Cause — 588 U.S. 684 (June 27, 2019)

**History and docket placement.** The actual Court vacated the judgment for challengers and ordered dismissal as nonjusticiable. The 2016 map followed a racial-gerrymandering judgment that may not survive this simulation; its use must therefore be labeled an **expressly reconstructed vehicle**, not inevitable history. [Actual opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/18-422).

**Questions.** Are the preserved partisan claims judicially cognizable under the proposed bounded rule, and does the record establish representation foreclosure across the historical range or a distinct material association injury with purpose and causation?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reject categorical nonjusticiability; vacate and remand for the proposed Foreclose elements, expressly adopting any necessary rule not yet established in the simulation, preserving supported subsidiary findings. Apply successor continuity only upon proof that the challenged assignments or injury persist. Examine representation and association separately. Entrench remains unavailable for a congressional delegation; neither an unchanged delegation tally nor the absence of a newly defeated incumbent defeats a properly proved Foreclose claim.

**Reasoning.** The deliberate ten-to-three target supplies purpose evidence, not a complete injury. Representation requires an identified electorate’s inability to elect in every destination throughout the recorded conditions over the prior lawful map’s life, with a lawful and factually connected baseline. Effective opportunity anywhere under a condition in that range defeats this branch. An emerging electorate may establish a real opportunity through an independently grounded lawful comparison without prior electoral success; the historical reference range remains unchanged. The association record includes recruitment, funding, volunteer, and mobilization problems. Determine whether a concrete districting mechanism materially impaired actual association or concrete efforts to organize or participate, rather than merely reducing political influence. The injury need not persist for a specified period, although its duration and timing bear on practical impairment and relief. [District opinion on remand, 318 F. Supp. 3d 777, 829–831, 931–933 (2018)](https://www.law.nyu.edu/sites/default/files/Common%20Cause%20v.%20Rucho.pdf). [*Gaffney*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/412/735) permits political awareness; [*Elrod*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/427/347) supports the association analogy. Comparison plans and continuity evidence test causation without promising proportional seats. The narrower congressional coverage remains a substantive limitation, not a claim that Foreclose and Entrench are interchangeable.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Reject categorical nonjusticiability and expressly adopt any necessary part of the bounded rule not already established. Separate the recorded-range representation inquiry from association injury; allow an independently grounded lawful comparison for an emerging electorate while retaining the historical range. Require proof for successor continuity and causal attribution, without a mandatory election count or statistical model. Association covers actual activity or concrete organizing and participation efforts, with no independent duration requirement. Preserve purpose and same-decision analysis, exclude congressional Entrench, and remand only preserved claims for violation-matched prospective relief.

#### October Term 2019

##### League of Women Voters of Michigan v. Benson — 373 F. Supp. 3d 867 (E.D. Mich. April 25, 2019); Supreme Court appeal: Chatfield v. League of Women Voters of Michigan, No. 19-220

**History and docket placement.** The three-judge court found unconstitutional partisan gerrymandering; the Supreme Court vacated and remanded in light of *Rucho* on **October 21, 2019**, placing the appellate action in OT2019. [Official docket](https://www.supremecourt.gov/docket/docketfiles/html/public/19-220.html).

**Questions.** Do the legislative maps prevent an opposing voting majority from replacing the governing party across the recorded electoral range? Do preserved congressional claims establish loss of effective opportunity or material interference with political association, and what relief follows from the proved placements?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Grant plenary review instead of the historical *Rucho* remand. Vacate and remand to apply Entrench separately to each legislative chamber and Foreclose to any preserved congressional claim. Retain supported findings, but do not infer liability from statewide vote shares. Reconsider the remedy’s schedule and necessary boundary changes in light of the injury actually established.

**Reasoning.** The trial evidence includes mapmaking records, Michigan’s statutory Apol criteria, electoral performance, standing, and alternatives. Under Entrench, determine whether the governing party retains control throughout recorded conditions in which opponents have an absolute vote majority, with reliable whole-body measurement and map causation. No fixed number of qualifying cycles is mandatory, and two observed inversions are not sufficient by themselves to establish the full range. Congressional Foreclose separately requires representation loss or material interference with actual association or concrete efforts to organize or participate; the association branch has no separate duration prerequisite. [District opinion](https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/michigan/miedce/2:2017cv14148/325954/268/). [*Gaffney*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/412/735) guards against proportionality as the rule; [*Reynolds*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/377/533) supports attention to election timing. Michigan elects its entire Senate for concurrent four-year terms; use its own whole-chamber record rather than importing House votes from intervening years. [Michigan Constitution, Article IV, §2](https://legislature.mi.gov/Laws/MCL?objectName=MCL-ARTICLE-IV-2). Reconcile uncontested-seat treatment before relying on aggregate vote percentages. Neither a special election nor a statewide redraw follows automatically from a district-specific injury.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify the chamber-specific Entrench inquiry, the absence of a fixed election-count gate, reliable treatment of uncontested seats, and map causation. Apply Foreclose separately to congressional claims, including material interference with existing association or concrete organizing efforts without a separate duration requirement. Preserve supported findings, require independently grounded lawful comparisons, and tie any redraw or special-election schedule to the actual injury.

#### October Term 2020

##### Brnovich v. Democratic National Committee

**History and docket placement.** Actual merits decision, July 1, 2021. The Court reversed the Ninth Circuit and upheld Arizona’s out-of-precinct rule and restrictions on third-party ballot collection under §2; it also rejected the intentional-discrimination determination. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/19-1257).

**Questions.** Do the out-of-precinct rule and ballot-collection restrictions leave protected voters with less opportunity to participate under §2 in Arizona’s actual electoral conditions? What weight should size of disparity, alternative voting methods, historical practice, and asserted state interests receive?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the Ninth Circuit’s §2 judgment against both rules and remand for appropriately limited relief. Resolve the statutory claims without making the separate constitutional intent question necessary to the judgment.

**Reasoning.** Section 2 asks whether the challenged practice, in its actual setting, leaves protected voters with less opportunity to participate and elect. The record connects precinct instability and unequal access to mail and transportation with the challenged restrictions. Counting only ballots cast at the correct precinct may serve administration, but discarding otherwise valid votes for statewide offices needs an explanation responsive to that exclusion. Likewise, ballot security does not by itself explain denying collection assistance on which affected voters depend. Neither a small statewide percentage nor the existence of other voting methods answers whether those voters can realistically use them. Reject a presumptive 1982 safe harbor while preserving a genuine totality inquiry. *Gingles* explains the interaction between electoral rules and existing conditions; *Chisom* confirms that the 1982 amendment created a results test. Neither requires importing districting preconditions into a vote-denial claim. [*Gingles*, 478 U.S. 30, 47 (1986)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/478/30); [*Chisom*, 501 U.S. 380, 394–398 (1991)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/501/380).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Clarify a practical, contextual §2 opportunity inquiry that preserves the plaintiffs’ statutory burden, rejects categorical immunity based on 1982 practice or small aggregate disparities, and evaluates the actual usability of alternatives and the State’s asserted interests. Reserve the unnecessary constitutional intent issue. Confine relief to the unlawful applications or practices established on the record.

#### October Term 2021

##### Wisconsin Legislature v. Wisconsin Elections Commission

**History and docket placement.** Actual emergency application converted into certiorari, March 23, 2022. The Court reversed the Wisconsin Supreme Court’s adoption of the Governor’s legislative maps because its justification for adding a seventh majority-Black Assembly district did not satisfy actual racial-gerrymandering doctrine. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/21A471).

**Questions.** Does creating an additional majority-Black opportunity district establish a constitutional deprivation unless §2 required that precise configuration? What distinct statutory and remedial-authority questions remain?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Deny the requested stay and summary reversal on the racial-design ground. Leave the adopted maps in place against that challenge.

**Reasoning.** The fictional *Shaw–Miller–Vera* rule does not convert intentional racial consideration in shared territorial districts into an independent constitutional injury. Adding an opportunity district therefore does not require proving that §2 compelled exactly that configuration merely to defeat a design-only challenge. The state court had an independent task: replacing malapportioned districts after legislative deadlock. This conclusion does not establish that every feature of its map is lawful or eliminate a separately pleaded dilution, access, population, or remedial-authority objection. The distinction is between permission to consider racial opportunity and a statutory duty to create a particular district. *Gingles* and *De Grandy* address the latter through opportunity and the totality of circumstances; they do not make every permissible map a mandatory remedy. [*Gingles* (1986)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/478/30); [*De Grandy*, 512 U.S. 997 (1994)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/512/997).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply the existing distinction between permissible racial consideration and an independent deprivation; do not announce a new entitlement to a seventh district. Clarify permission versus statutory compulsion. Preserve any separately presented dilution, access, population, or remedial-authority issue, and state that denying this design challenge does not approve every feature of the map.

#### October Term 2022

##### Allen v. Milligan

**History and docket placement.** Actual merits decision, June 8, 2023. The Court affirmed preliminary injunctions because the plaintiffs demonstrated a likelihood of success on their §2 challenges to Alabama’s congressional map. This was review of preliminary relief, not a final trial judgment. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/21-1086).

**Questions.** Did the plaintiffs establish likely §2 dilution, including a reasonably configured opportunity district and the required local voting conditions? Must their illustrative maps satisfy Alabama’s proposed race-neutral benchmark?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm the preliminary injunctions and allow Alabama the first opportunity to enact an effective lawful remedy.

**Reasoning.** A reasonably configured additional opportunity district, demonstrated cohesion and bloc voting, and the full local record support likely statutory dilution. Race-conscious illustrative maps can show a practicable remedy without making racial proportionality the legal entitlement. Alabama’s proposed race-neutral benchmark would make the State’s preferred assumptions determine whether the inequality could be recognized at all. The fictional *Holder* principle instead requires an independently justified, workable comparator; it does not require every comparison to ignore race. The real *Gingles* opportunity analysis and *De Grandy* rejection of mechanical proportionality supply useful parallels, while fictional *Valdespino* preserves crossover opportunities beyond the numerical-majority facts actually necessary here. [*Gingles* (1986)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/478/30); [*De Grandy*, 512 U.S. 997, 1011–1018 (1994)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/512/997).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply the independent §2 prerequisites and totality inquiry at the preliminary-injunction standard. Clarify that a workable illustrative map may consider race without establishing a right to proportional seats. Keep the constitutional operations separate from §2, distinguish likely success from final liability, and give Alabama the first opportunity to provide an effective lawful cure.

##### Moore v. Harper

**History and docket placement.** Actual merits decision, June 27, 2023. The Court retained jurisdiction despite intervening state proceedings and rejected the claim that the Elections Clause frees state legislatures from ordinary state constitutional review. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/21-1271).

**Questions.** Does the Elections Clause exempt congressional redistricting from ordinary state constitutional review? What federal limits remain on a state court’s exercise of that review?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Affirm on the Elections Clause question. Preserve review of an identified federal limitation without treating disagreement with state-law interpretation as sufficient.

**Reasoning.** A legislature exercising a lawmaking function receives that authority within its State’s constitutional system. *Hildebrant* sustained a referendum’s place in congressional redistricting; *Smiley* required compliance with the ordinary gubernatorial veto. Judicial enforcement of the state constitution follows the same institutional principle. A legislature cannot invoke a federal assignment of legislative responsibility to discard the legal framework that defines its lawmaking acts. This does not authorize state courts to replace ordinary interpretation with unrestricted election policymaking. Nor does the case decide whether a particular partisan map violates Stone’s proposed federal test: the state court’s authority and the substantive rule governing its judgment are separate questions. [*Ohio ex rel. Davis v. Hildebrant*, 241 U.S. 565 (1916)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/241/565); [*Smiley v. Holm*, 285 U.S. 355, 367–373 (1932)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/285/355).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply the State’s ordinary constitutional lawmaking framework to the Elections Clause. Explain the retained federal boundary without converting routine disagreement over state law into federal error. Reserve the distinct substantive constitutionality of any particular partisan map; affirmance on institutional authority does not resolve it.

#### October Term 2023

##### Alexander v. South Carolina State Conference of the NAACP

**History and docket placement.** Actual merits decision, May 23, 2024. The Court reversed the racial-gerrymandering determination and remanded the distinct intentional-dilution claim. [Opinion, Part IV](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/22-807).

**Questions.** Does racial predominance establish constitutional injury? Was there separately proved intentional impairment of electoral opportunity, and does any preserved coalition or participation claim require an independent analysis?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reverse relief resting solely on racial predominance; vacate and remand the separate dilution determination for injury-specific findings under the fictional *Easley* line.

**Reasoning.** Race-conscious district design does not itself establish the fictional constitutional wrong. But a partisan explanation does not excuse proven racial impairment. The district court must determine whether racial purpose actually contributed to materially diminished electoral opportunity, rather than carry over its predominance finding as the answer to both claims. Ordinary review of factual findings remains applicable; the court should not require an alternative map that preserves the challenged partisan advantage as the exclusive means of proving purpose. *Arlington Heights* supports using direct and circumstantial evidence; *Gomillion* shows that boundary power remains subject to independently established voting rights. Neither supplies an automatic win. [*Arlington Heights*, 429 U.S. 252, 265–268 (1977)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/252); [*Gomillion*, 364 U.S. 339 (1960)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/364/339).

The proposed racial Burden rule reaches concrete coalition and participation injury. Apply that ground only if procedurally preserved, protecting realistic coalition-building as well as existing associations. A surviving preferred candidate does not by itself defeat that distinct injury. Dilute independently requires material electoral-opportunity impairment, without a completed-election prerequisite or a guaranteed winning map. No new claim is manufactured on remand.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Separate racial design, intentional dilution, and any preserved racial Burden claim. Require findings on prohibited purpose, concrete injury, and causation, with ordinary factual review and the applicable same-decision defense. Recognize realistic coalition-building and participation without requiring loss of a winnable district; reserve unpresented claims and avoid manufacturing a new count on remand.

#### October Term 2024

##### Louisiana v. Callais — argument and carryover

**History and docket placement.** The Court first heard argument on March 24, 2025, and ordered reargument on June 27, 2025. Its merits judgment belongs in OT2025. [Official docket, No. 24-109](https://www.supremecourt.gov/docket/docketfiles/html/public/24-109.html).

**Questions.** Does legislative reliance on racial opportunity in responding to §2 litigation establish an independent constitutional injury, and how does §2’s results standard bear on the State’s asserted justification?

**Stone’s recommended use.** Retain this procedural carryover, with the substantive position recorded under OT2025. Reargument is a procedural event, not an additional holding or an occasion to assume Stone joined the real Court’s order.

**Reasoning.** Reargument preserves a pending merits question. Its occurrence does not itself establish a substantive rule or reveal Stone’s position on the ultimate judgment.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Record the procedural carryover without announcing a separate merits holding or attributing an unverified vote to Stone. Reserve the substantive disposition for OT2025 and keep the actual reargument event distinct from the proposed fictional merits result.

#### October Term 2025

##### Abbott v. League of United Latin American Citizens — Texas’s 2025 map

**History and docket placement.** The Court stayed the district court’s injunction on December 4, 2025, then summarily reversed on April 27, 2026. The real rulings rested on racial-predominance proof, deference to legislative explanations, and election timing. The case was therefore no longer merely an unresolved emergency application by November 2026. [December opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/25A608); [merits docket, No. 25-845](https://www.supremecourt.gov/docket/docketfiles/html/public/25-845.html).

**Questions.** Can racial predominance alone sustain the injunction? Does a preserved independent racial-deprivation claim remain, and what findings or defenses require further consideration?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Vacate the injunction insofar as racial predominance alone supplies liability and remand only an actually presented and preserved independent racial deprivation requiring further decision. Preserve relevant fact findings; the remand creates no entitlement to a new count and partisanship supplies no categorical immunity.

**Reasoning.** The fictional *Shaw* line rejects the actual design-only theory. The fictional *Easley* line equally rejects using partisanship as immunity from purposeful racial impairment. *Arlington Heights* permits a discriminatory purpose to be one motivating factor; a genuine same-decision defense addresses causation rather than relabeling the purpose. Relief must track an injury actually pleaded and proved. [*Arlington Heights* (1977)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/252); [*Mt. Healthy*, 429 U.S. 274, 285–287 (1977)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/274).

A properly presented Foreclose claim can proceed before voting under the proposed Foreclose rule. Representation requires loss throughout the historical range; association requires material interference with actual political activity or concrete efforts to organize or participate, without a separate duration prerequisite. Purpose, causation, continuity, and the same-decision defense remain separate. Congressional Entrench is excluded. Neither that doctrine nor the racial appeal creates an unpleaded partisan count.

**What the opinion must articulate.** Reject design-only liability while preserving supported facts relevant to an actually preserved deprivation. Explain motivating purpose and the genuine same-decision defense; partisanship is neither immunity nor proof of racial injury. Any separately presented Foreclose claim may arise before voting, with distinct representation and association elements and separate interim-relief findings. Reserve unpleaded counts; congressional Entrench remains excluded.

##### Tangipa v. Newsom — California’s Proposition 50 map

**History and docket placement.** Actual emergency application denied February 4, 2026. The applicants sought an injunction against California’s new congressional map; the denial did not decide its ultimate constitutionality. [Official docket, No. 25A839](https://www.supremecourt.gov/docket/docketfiles/html/public/25a839.html).

**Questions.** Does the presented racial-design claim establish grounds for emergency relief? Do referendum approval or a partisan justification alter the requirements for a separately presented constitutional deprivation?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Deny an emergency injunction on a design-only racial theory. Preserve separately supported constitutional or statutory claims in the ordinary proceedings.

**Reasoning.** The same fictional *Shaw* rule applies to California and Texas: drawing boundaries with racial awareness is insufficient, while an independently proved deprivation remains actionable. Voter approval establishes a method of lawmaking, not immunity from federal rights. *Hildebrant* recognizes a referendum’s institutional role; *Gomillion* establishes the separate limit imposed by voting rights. Neither case says that a map adopted to offset another State’s partisan advantage is therefore lawful or unlawful. [*Hildebrant* (1916)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/241/565); [*Gomillion* (1960)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/364/339).

The proposed Foreclose rule permits a properly pleaded Foreclose claim before an election, using reliable evidence rather than a mandatory method. A retaliatory explanation supplies purpose evidence, not proof of representation foreclosure or material impairment of association or concrete organizing efforts. Apply the same rule as in Texas, with no automatic constitutional exemption for referendum approval or offsetting another State’s advantage. Congressional Entrench remains excluded.

**What the opinion must articulate.** State the emergency disposition’s limited scope. Apply the same independent-deprivation rule to maps adopted by referendum and by a legislature. For any properly presented Foreclose claim, explain purpose, injury, causation, and the same-decision defense without an election-count gate or a referendum exemption. Preserve ordinary proceedings on separate claims and state Entrench’s exclusion of congressional delegations.

##### Louisiana v. Callais — racial design and §2

**History and docket placement.** Actual decision April 29, 2026, after October 15, 2025 reargument. The Court upheld invalidation of Louisiana’s second majority-Black district. It also construed §2 districting liability to require circumstances supporting a strong inference of intentional racial discrimination. [Opinion](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/24-109_2026-04-29).

**Questions.** Does the consideration of race in Louisiana’s replacement map itself establish a constitutional violation? Must a §2 results claimant additionally establish circumstances supporting an inference of intentional racial discrimination?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Reverse the judgment invalidating the map on the racial-design theory. Preserve the fictional §2 results framework rather than import the actual Court’s new intent requirement.

**Reasoning.** Under fictional *Shaw–Miller–Vera*, racial consideration in a shared territorial district is not the independent injury asserted. Statutory permission and statutory compulsion also differ: failure to show that §2 required this precise map would not itself establish a constitutional prohibition. On §2, Congress deliberately adopted a results standard, while the fictional enforcement-power line permits appropriately fitted prevention of constitutional wrongs. A private plaintiff proves the valid statute’s elements, not an additional personal constitutional violation. *Chisom* supplies the historical reading of the 1982 amendment; *City of Rome* illustrates permissible effects-based prevention under the Fifteenth Amendment, though its §5 setting does not decide every §2 application. [*Chisom*, 501 U.S. 380, 394 (1991)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/501/380); [*City of Rome*, 446 U.S. 156, 173–178 (1980)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/446/156).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply the existing rejection of design-only constitutional injury and retain §2’s independent results standard. Explain the statutory and preventive-enforcement basis without treating every racial consideration as either mandatory or forbidden. This judgment does not adjudicate an unpleaded Foreclose claim or declare every aspect of the map valid.

##### Allen v. Milligan — 2026 emergency sequel

**History and docket placement.** On June 2, 2026, the Court stayed renewed injunctions against Alabama’s 2023 map after remands following *Callais*. The order covered *Milligan*, *Singleton*, and *Caster*. [Opinion, Nos. 25A1314–1316](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/25A1314).

The actual *Callais*-triggered remand would not occur identically under Stone’s proposed result. The emergency sequel is therefore retained as an expressly reconstructed vehicle using the actual later record, not an inevitable procedural consequence of the fictional judgment.

**Questions.** Should the renewed injunctions remain operative during merits review under the continuing §2 results standard? How do the later map, separate constitutional findings, and practical election disruption affect the stay requests?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Deny the State’s requested stays, retaining the operative remedial protection while the merits appeals proceed.

**Reasoning.** Stone’s recommended doctrinal sequence rejects *Callais*’s new restriction of §2. Its fictional *Milligan* judgment therefore continues to support effective statutory relief, subject to the record concerning the later map. A State’s legal disagreement with a remedy is not automatically discriminatory intent, but an actual course of deliberate evasion can be evidence alongside the other *Arlington Heights* factors. The court must distinguish the statutory ground from the separate constitutional findings. Election stability should examine which map voters and officials have been using and what disruption each proposed change would cause; it does not automatically favor the most recent legislative enactment. Historical *Upham* supports deference to lawful state choices while requiring correction of the violation. [*Arlington Heights* (1977)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/252); [*Upham v. Seamon*, 456 U.S. 37, 43 (1982)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/456/37).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Distinguish the statutory ground from separate findings of intentional discrimination; identify the operative remedy and the disruption each proposed change would cause. Apply stay requirements to the later record, preserve effective relief pending review, and explain the reconstructed procedural premise created by rejecting the actual Callais rule.

#### October Term 2026

##### Allen v. Milligan — pending merits appeal

**History and docket placement.** As checked November 10, 2026, No. 26-117 remained pending. The official docket’s November 9 entry redistributed it for the November 13 conference; it supplies no final merits disposition. [Official docket](https://www.supremecourt.gov/docket/docketfiles/html/public/26-117.html).

**Questions.** Do the later Alabama map and renewed injunctions satisfy the statutory and constitutional rules? What review and interim protection are appropriate while the merits appeal remains pending?

**Stone’s recommended disposition.** Note probable jurisdiction and hear the merits with the related litigation as procedurally appropriate. Retain the remedial protection pending review; no future merits result is assumed.

**Reasoning.** The issue is whether the later Alabama map and the renewed injunction satisfy the governing statutory and constitutional rules, not whether a real-world *Callais* restriction automatically controls the fictional Court. Review the operative findings, the State’s claimed lawful criteria, and the scope of relief separately. *Gingles* supplies the statutory opportunity inquiry; *Arlington Heights* supplies the distinct purpose inquiry. The completed trial record can support disposition, but scheduling review does not establish approval of either party’s merits position. [*Gingles* (1986)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/478/30); [*Arlington Heights* (1977)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/252).

**What the opinion must articulate.** If plenary review is ordered, identify the questions accepted and the governing interim order without announcing a future merits result. Preserve separate statutory and constitutional inquiries, assess the later findings and remedial scope, and distinguish the actual pending docket from Stone’s recommended procedural action.

##### Texas and California — post-election partisan applications

**History and docket placement.** Hypothetical applications; no case captions or Supreme Court grants are verified. The November 3 election has occurred, but press-based seat counts do not establish legal injury. California’s official timetable provides for statewide certification December 11; its canvass was continuing as of November 10, 2026. Texas’s live results page is election reporting, not proof here of final adjudicated results. [California official timetable](https://www.sos.ca.gov/elections/upcoming-elections/general-election-november-3-2026); [Texas results](https://electionresults.sos.state.tx.us/results.html).

**Questions.** Do proper plaintiffs establish representation foreclosure across the recorded range, or concrete impairment of association or organizing efforts, under the operative maps? How should later election evidence affect liability and continuing relief?

**Stone’s recommended use.** Apply Foreclose without awaiting final returns as a prerequisite to accrual. If election outcomes are relied on as completed-election proof, verify their finality and relevance. Representation requires an identified electorate and loss throughout the historical range, with supported continuity or an emerging opportunity established through an independently grounded lawful comparison. Association requires its separate concrete mechanism and material impairment of actual political activity or concrete organizing efforts; no separate duration prerequisite applies. Prohibited purpose, causation, and the State’s same-decision defense remain necessary. Neither reported seat changes nor every destination’s defeat in a single election establishes the representation element. Reported seat changes cannot be converted into a count of constitutional violations.

**Reasoning.** *Elrod* supports protection against governmental penalties for political association without establishing a right to unchanged districts; *Mt. Healthy* supplies a causal safeguard. Apply the proposed extension symmetrically to identified voters. A demonstrated opportunity under the challenged map within the selected range defeats representation foreclosure, but does not automatically defeat a separate association claim. Success under a remedial map does not prove the original map lawful. Material later evidence is assessed through ordinary proceedings for continuing or modifying relief, not an automatic dissolution rule. [*Elrod*, 427 U.S. 347 (1976)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/427/347); [*Mt. Healthy*](https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/429/274).

**What the opinion must articulate.** Apply A-prime’s complete-loss inquiry across the recorded range, including supported lawful-map continuity and independently grounded emerging-opportunity comparisons. Apply the association branch independently to material impairment of actual political activity or concrete organizing efforts, without a separate duration gate. Identify personal injury, prohibited purpose, causation, and the State’s same-decision defense. Verify any election results used as completed-election evidence; use ordinary modification proceedings, not automatic dissolution, and prescribe only a violation-matched remedy.

#### Remaining limits and reserved vehicles

- No identified vehicle supplies an actual racial ballot-designation record for adopting every proposed **Mark** rule. Historical *Anderson v. Martin*, 375 U.S. 399 (1964), is a relevant racial-ballot-label precedent, not a reason to make an unnecessary holding in *Cano*. [Opinion](https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/375/399/).
- No entry establishes a successful **Segregate** claim. Adoption of that new operation and proof of its elements are separate decisions.
- No identified case establishes an **Entrench** judgment against a city or county governing body. *Osceola County* is a statutory at-large dilution case, not a substitute.
- An OT2026 post-election application requires an identified lawsuit, operative map, proper parties, and the evidence required by the selected doctrine. The date of an election and a reported seat count do not supply a judgment.

---

<a id="carried-forward-plans"></a>

### 6.3 Earlier plans outside the main voting docket

These entries preserve the tracker’s older plans for OT2000 and for four OT2001 matters outside the principal voting docket. They have been reorganized, not rerun as new case briefs. The original tracker used a June 29, 2000 fictional-law baseline; importing these plans does not reconcile that snapshot with later decisions in the supplied holdings register. Before using any entry, check whether the fictional Court has since decided it. An existing fictional judgment controls over an older proposal. No colleague’s vote or opinion assignment is supplied.

The questions below are editorial summaries, not quotations of official questions presented. Where an earlier plan conflicts with a recorded position, the conflict remains explicit. A carried-forward recommendation is not a new holding or an automatic instruction to reopen a completed case.

#### October Term 2000 — carried-forward plans

##### Sinkfield v. Kelley — personal injury and neighboring districts

**History and docket placement.** The tracker places this historical November 27, 2000 decision on its OT2000 list. Its older planning discussion concerns standing, not an adjudication of all independent districting injuries.

**Questions.** May voters in districts neighboring the challenged districts sue for dilution or another injury to their own voting rights? How does personal exposure differ from an objection to someone else’s district composition?

**Stone’s carried-forward recommendation.** Resolve standing by the injury personally alleged: neighboring-district residence does not defeat a plaintiff’s own dilution claim, but a composition-only objection to another district does not establish that injury. The earlier plan supplies no verified judgment-specific disposition beyond this proposed standing rule.
